6
Conclusion and possible extensions

In this paper, we proposed a new approach to estimate an non-randomized treatment effect at the
\sqrt{n}
rate and showed that under homoscedastic normal errors our method is semiparametrically efficient. We also pointed out in Remark
3.8
that one can use weighted least squares instead of ordinary least squares to take care of heterogenous errors. However, the normality assumption is necessary for semiparametric efficiency as we use least squares for estimating the treatment effect. We now discuss some natural extensions of our models that are worth analyzing as potential future research problems.

6.1
Non-constant treatment effect

Consider the following extension of our model with non-constant treatment effect:

\displaystyle Y_{i}

\displaystyle=\alpha(\eta_{i})\mathds{1}_{Q_{i}>0}+X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}+\nu_{i}=\alpha(\eta_{i})\mathds{1}_{Q_{i}>0}+X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}+b(\eta_{i})+{\epsilon}_{i}

(6.1)

\displaystyle Q_{i}

\displaystyle=Z_{i}^{\top}\gamma_{0}+\eta_{i}\,.

(6.2)

where as before
b(\eta_{i})=\mathbb{E}[\nu\mid\eta_{i}]
. This generalization assumes that the response of a treated candidate with higher abilities is boosted in comparison to another treated candidate with lower ability. As an example, a more capable student upon entering into a prestigious grad school, will most likely get a better mentor, resulting in an amplification of their academic prowess. The random variable
\alpha(\eta)
represents the
conditional treatment effect
as:

\mathbb{E}[Y\mid X,\eta,Q>0]-\mathbb{E}[Y\mid X,\eta,Q<0]=\alpha(\eta)\,.

i.e. conditioning on
(X,\eta)
(background information and innate ability),
\alpha(\eta)
quantifies the difference between the responses of treated and untreated samples.
It can be shown that the estimator proposed in our manuscript based on the model with constant
\alpha_{0}
, estimates, in the newly proposed model, a
weighted average
of
\alpha(\eta)
, i.e.
\mathbb{E}[p(\eta)\alpha(\eta)]/\mathbb{E}[p(\eta)]
where
p(\cdot)
is a non-negative integrable function depending on other parameters. This weight function is basically a function of the information for
\alpha
in each
\eta
- stratum. So our current method is applicable when the parameter of interest is a
weighted average treatment effect
.

However if the parameter of interest is
unweighted average treatment effect

\mathbb{E}[\alpha(\eta)]
, then our method can be slightly modified as follows to yield a
\sqrt{n}
-consistent estimator:

1.

Split the data into three (almost) equal parts say
\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2},\mathcal{D}_{3}
.

2.

From
\mathcal{D}_{1}
, impute
\hat{\eta}
from equation (
6.2
) by regressing
Q
on
X
.

3.

Estimate
\alpha(\cdot)
and
b(\cdot)
(and their derivatives) from
\mathcal{D}_{2}
using a B-spline series expansion.

4.

Note that equation (
6.1
) can be expanded as:

\displaystyle Y_{i}

\displaystyle=\alpha(\eta_{i})\mathds{1}_{Q_{i}>0}+X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}+b(\eta_{i})+{\epsilon}_{i}

\displaystyle=\alpha(\hat{\eta}_{i})\mathds{1}_{Q_{i}>0}+b(\hat{\eta}_{i})+X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}+(\hat{\gamma}-\gamma_{0})^{\top}Z_{i}\left(\alpha^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i})\mathds{1}_{Q_{i}>0}+b^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i})\right)+{\epsilon}_{i}+R_{i}

\displaystyle\approx\alpha(\hat{\eta}_{i})\mathds{1}_{Q_{i}>0}+b(\hat{\eta}_{i})+X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}+(\hat{\gamma}-\gamma_{0})^{\top}Z_{i}\left(\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i})\mathds{1}_{Q_{i}>0}+\hat{b}^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i})\right)+{\epsilon}_{i}+R_{i}

where in the last line we replaced
\alpha^{\prime}
and
b^{\prime}
by their estimates obtained from
\mathcal{D}_{2}
in the previous step. Finally, we can re-estimate
\alpha
from the above equation via a non-parametric method (i.e. expanding via a B-spline basis and regressing
Y
on an appropriate set of covariates). Our final estimator becomes:

\hat{\mathbb{E}}[\alpha(\eta)]=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{\alpha}(\hat{\eta}_{i})\,.

An analysis similar to that in our paper indicates that this estimator has
\sqrt{n}
rate of convergence and is asymptotically normal. However whether this is semi-parametrically efficient is not clear at this moment and a potentially interesting problem for future research.

6.2
Bootstrap consistency

As noted in Remark
5.1
, we use a bootstrap confidence interval instead of the asymptotic one owing to the intricate form of the asymptotic variance of our estimator, which makes it hard to estimate from the data. Therefore, an immediate question of interest is to investigate whether the bootstrap is consistent under our model assumptions. Although empirical evidence suggests that this is the case, a rigorous theoretical undertaking is essential to establish the claim.

Appendix A
Proof of Theorem 4.10

Proof.

For the ease of notation, we assume
\dim(X)=p_{1}\asymp\dim(Z)=p_{2}\asymp p
. One can extend our proof quite easily for general
p_{1},p_{2}
(i.e. when they are not of same order) with a careful booking for the dimension factor. The entire proof is quite long and tedious, therefore in this appendix we will state the key steps and provide proofs of the main parts. Proofs of all supplementary lemmas and propositions can be found in Appendix.

Recall that our estimation procedure consists of three parts:

•

Estimate
\gamma_{0}
from
\mathcal{D}_{1}
.

•

Estimate
b^{\prime}
from
\mathcal{D}_{2}
.

•

Estimate
\alpha_{0}
from
\mathcal{D}_{3}
.

A.1
Estimation of
\hat{\gamma}_{0}
from
\mathcal{D}_{1}

This part is the easiest among all the three parts. We estimate
\gamma_{0}
by doing LASSO of
\mathbf{Y}
in
\mathbf{Z}
. Note that, by sub-gaussianity assumption,
\mathbf{Z}
satisfies restricted eigenvalue condition with high probability. Therefore, by standard lasso calculation with the tuning parameter
\lambda\asymp\sqrt{\log{p}/n}
we have:

\displaystyle\left\|\hat{\gamma}-\gamma_{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}\,,

\displaystyle\left\|\hat{\gamma}-\gamma_{0}\right\|_{1}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}s_{\gamma}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}\,.

We will use this estimator in the subsequent analysis.

A.2
Estimation of
b^{\prime}
from
\mathcal{D}_{2}

In this subsection, we present the estimation error for
b^{\prime}
. An in our fixed dimensional analysis, we expand the model equation as:

\mathbf{Y}=\mathbf{X}\beta_{0}+\mathbf{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})\omega_{b}+\mathbf{R}_{1}+\mathbf{R}_{2}+{\bm{\epsilon}}\,.

where
\mathbf{N}_{K}(\hat{\eta})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times K}
with
\mathbf{N}_{K}(\hat{\eta})_{i,j}=\tilde{N}_{K,j}(\hat{\eta}_{i})
,
N_{K,j}
being the scaled
K^{th}
B-spline basis (see Section
F
of the supplementary document for details). Here
\omega_{b}
is the coefficient of
best B-spline approximator
of
b
with respect to
K
basis. Our aim is to estimate
\omega_{b}
from the data, as upon obtaining
\hat{\omega}_{b}
one can define
\hat{b}^{\prime}(t)=\nabla N_{K}(t)^{\top}\hat{\omega}_{b}
, where
\nabla\tilde{N}_{K}(t)
is the vector of derivatives of B-spline basis. Therefore the estimation error
\hat{b}^{\prime}
can be bounded as:

\sup_{|t|\leq\tau}\left|b^{\prime}(t)-\hat{b}^{\prime}(t)\right|\leq\sup_{|t|\leq\tau}\left\|\nabla\tilde{N}_{k}(t)\right\|\times\left\|\hat{\omega}_{b}-\omega_{b}\right\|+\sup_{|t|\leq\tau}\left|b^{\prime}(t)-\tilde{N}_{K}(t)^{\top}\omega_{b}\right|\,.

The second term is the B-spline approximation error, which is bounded by the order of
K^{-(\upsilon-1)}
(see Theorem
F.1
of the supplementary document). For the first term or estimation error, as mentioned in Section
F
, we have
\sup_{|t|\leq\tau}\|\nabla\tilde{N}_{k}(t)\|\lesssim K^{3/2}
. This further implies:

\sup_{|t|\leq\tau}\left|b^{\prime}(t)-\hat{b}^{\prime}(t)\right|\lesssim K^{3/2}\left\|\hat{\omega}_{b}-\omega_{b}\right\|+K^{-(\upsilon-1)}\,,

and consequently, the estimation error
\hat{b}^{\prime}
completely depends on the estimation error of
\hat{\omega}_{b}
. In the proposition we present a bound on the estimation error on
\hat{\omega}_{b}
(and consequently on
\hat{b}^{\prime}
):

Proposition A.1
.

Under the Assumptions stated in Section
4
, we have:

\left\|\hat{\omega}_{b}-\omega_{b}\right\|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\sqrt{K}s_{\beta}\left(\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}+K^{-2\upsilon}\right)+K^{-\upsilon}+\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{K}{n}}

and consequently:

\sup_{|t|\leq\tau}\left|b^{\prime}(t)-\hat{b}^{\prime}(t)\right|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{n}{s^{2}_{\beta}s_{\gamma}\log{p}}\right)^{\frac{-(\upsilon-\frac{3}{2})}{2\upsilon+1}}\,.

Proof of Proposition
A.1
.

The method is, first we estimate
\beta_{0}
using LASSO as follows:

\hat{\beta}=\mathop{\rm argmin}_{\beta}\left\{\frac{1}{2n}\left\|P_{\mathbf{N}_{k}}^{\perp}\left(\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{X}\beta\right)\right\|^{2}+\lambda\|\beta\|_{1}\right\}

Next we estimate
\omega_{b}
is:

\displaystyle\hat{\omega}_{b}=\left(\frac{\mathbf{N}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{N}_{k}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\mathbf{N}_{k}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{X}\hat{\beta}\right)}{n}

(A.1)

Therefore, to establish a bound on
\|\hat{\omega}_{b}-\omega_{b}\|
, a bound on the estimation error
\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0}
is necessary, which is established in the the following lemma:

Lemma A.2
.

Under our assumptions, we have the the following bound on the estimation error of
\beta_{0}
:

\left\|\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}s_{\beta}\left(\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}+K^{-2\upsilon}\right)^{2}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\frac{s_{\beta}s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}+K^{-4\upsilon}\triangleq r_{n,\beta_{0}}^{2}\,,

where
K
is the number of B-spline basis used for extending
b
.

Proof of Lemma
A.2
.

By the basic LASSO inequality, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n}\left\|P_{\mathbf{N}_{k}}^{\perp}\left(\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{X}\hat{\beta}\right)\right\|^{2}+\lambda\|\hat{\beta}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2n}\left\|P_{\mathbf{N}_{k}}^{\perp}\left(\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{X}\beta_{0}\right)\right\|^{2}+\lambda\|\beta_{0}\|_{1}

Some algebraic manipulation yields:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n}\left\|P_{\mathbf{N}_{k}}^{\perp}\mathbf{X}\left(\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0}\right)\right\|^{2}+\lambda\|\hat{\beta}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\left\|\left({\bm{\epsilon}}+\mathbf{R}\right)^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{k}}^{\perp}\mathbf{X}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0}\right\|_{1}+\lambda\|\beta_{0}\|_{1}

The matrix
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}^{\perp}\mathbf{X}
satisfies RE condition with high probability due to Assumption
4.5
and Proposition
A.5
. For the optimal value of
\lambda
, we need a bound on
(1/n)\|\left({\bm{\epsilon}}+\mathbf{R}\right)^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{k}}^{\perp}\mathbf{X}\|_{\infty}
, for which we bound
(1/n)\|{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{k}}^{\perp}\mathbf{X}\|_{\infty}
and
(1/n)\|\mathbf{R}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{k}}^{\perp}\mathbf{X}\|_{\infty}
separately. To bound the term with
{\bm{\epsilon}}
, we use the sub-gaussian concentration inequality:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{n}\left|{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{k}}^{\perp}\mathbf{X}_{*,j}\right|>t\mid\sigma(X,Z,\eta,\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2{\sf exp}{\left(-C\frac{nt^{2}}{\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{X}_{*,j}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{k}}^{\perp}\mathbf{X}_{*,j}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq 2{\sf exp}{\left(-C\frac{nt^{2}}{\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{X}_{*,j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{*,j}}\right)}

Now from the sub-gaussianity of
X_{j}
(with sub-gaussianity constant
\sigma_{W}
) applying Lemma
I.2
, we have probability going to
1
:

\max_{1\leq j\leq n}\frac{\|\mathbf{X}_{*,j}\|^{2}}{n}\leq 3\sigma_{W}\,.

Define the above event as
\Omega_{n}
. Using this we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{n}\left|{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{k}}^{\perp}\mathbf{X}_{*,j}\right|>t\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{n}\left|{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{k}}^{\perp}\mathbf{X}_{*,j}\right|>t,\Omega_{n}\right)+o(1)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{n}\left|{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{k}}^{\perp}\mathbf{X}_{*,j}\right|>t\mid\sigma(X,Z,\eta,\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)\right]+o(1)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}}{\sf exp}{\left(-C\frac{nt^{2}}{\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{X}_{*,j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{*,j}}\right)}\right]+o(1)

\displaystyle\leq 2{\sf exp}{\left(-C\frac{nt^{2}}{3\sigma_{W}^{2}}\right)}+o(1)

Therefore an appropriate choice of
t
yields:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\|{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}\,.

(A.2)

Now for the remainder term, recall that the remainder term
\mathbf{R}
consists of
\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{R}_{1}+\mathbf{R}_{2}
where

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{1,i}

\displaystyle=b(\eta_{i})-b(\hat{\eta}_{i})

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{2,i}

\displaystyle=b(\hat{\eta}_{i})-\mathbf{N}_{K}(\hat{\eta}_{i})^{\top}\omega_{b}\,.

We now bound these two remainder terms separately. For the first remainder term:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\|\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}^{\perp}\mathbf{X}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{1\leq j\leq p}\frac{1}{n}\left|\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}^{\perp}\mathbf{X}_{*,j}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{R}}\times\sqrt{\max_{1\leq j\leq p}\frac{1}{n}\|\mathbf{X}_{*,j}\|^{2}}

(A.3)

Again it follows from Lemma
I.2
that the second term of the above inequality is
O_{p}(1)
based on the subgaussianity of
\mathbf{X}_{*,j}
. For the first term, note that as
b
is Lipschitz (Assumption
4.1
), we have:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{R}_{1,i}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(b(\hat{\eta}_{i})-b(\eta_{i})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\hat{\eta}_{i}-\eta_{i}\right)^{2}\hskip 14.45377pt[\text{As }b\text{ is Lipschitz}]

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\right\|^{2}\times\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(Z_{i}^{\top}a_{n}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}\,.

where the last line follows from the fact that
\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|^{2}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}(s_{\gamma}\log{p})/n
(from LASSO on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
) and
(1/n)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(Z_{i}^{\top}a_{n}\right)^{2}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}1
follows from subgaussianity of
Z_{i}
along with Lemma
I.2
. Using this bound in equation (
A.3
) we conclude:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\|\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}^{\perp}\mathbf{X}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}\,.

(A.4)

For the other remainder term, we expand
\mathbf{X}_{*,j}
as:

\mathbf{X}_{*,j}=\mathbf{X}_{*,j}-\check{m}_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{\eta}})+\check{m}_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{\eta}})=\mathbf{X}_{*,j}-\check{m}_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{\eta}})+\mathbf{N}_{K}(\hat{\eta})w_{j}+\mathbf{R}_{j}

This implies:

\mathbf{R}_{2}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}^{\perp}\mathbf{X}_{*,j}=\mathbf{R}_{2}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}^{\perp}\left(\mathbf{X}_{*,j}-\check{m}_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{\eta}})\right)+\mathbf{R}_{2}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}^{\perp}\mathbf{R}_{j}\,.

As both
\mathbf{R}_{2}
and
P_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}^{\perp}
are function of
\hat{\eta}
, we have from the sub-gaussian concentration bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{n}\left|\mathbf{R}_{2}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}^{\perp}\left(\mathbf{X}_{*,j}-\check{m}_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{\eta}})\right)\right|>t\mid\hat{\eta}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2{\sf exp}{\left(-C\frac{nt^{2}}{\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{2}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}^{\perp}\mathbf{R}_{2}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq 2{\sf exp}{\left(-C\frac{nt^{2}}{\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{2}}\right)}\,.

From the spline approximation error,
(1/n)\mathbf{R}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{2}\leq K^{-2\upsilon}
(where
K
is the number of basis and
\upsilon
is the smoothness index of
b
(see Assumption
4.1
). Using this we have:

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{n}\left|\mathbf{R}_{2}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}^{\perp}\left(\mathbf{X}_{*,j}-\check{m}_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{\eta}})\right)\right|>t\mid\hat{\eta}\right)\leq 2{\sf exp}{\left(-C\frac{nt^{2}}{K^{-2\upsilon}}\right)}

which, upon unconditioning, taking union bound and choosing a suitable value of
t
yields:

\max_{1\leq j\leq p}\frac{1}{n}\left|\mathbf{R}_{2}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}^{\perp}\left(\mathbf{X}_{*,j}-\check{m}_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{\eta}})\right)\right|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}K^{-\upsilon}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}\,.

For the other remainder term, i.e.
(1/n)\mathbf{R}_{2}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}^{\perp}\mathbf{R}_{j}
, an application of Cauchy-Schwarz inequality yields:

\frac{1}{n}\left|\mathbf{R}_{2}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}^{\perp}\mathbf{R}_{j}\right|\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{2}}\times\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{j}}\leq K^{-2\upsilon}\,.

Therefore we have:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\|\mathbf{R}_{2}^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}^{\perp}\mathbf{X}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}K^{-\upsilon}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}+K^{-2\upsilon}\,.

(A.5)

Combining the bounds in equation (
A.2
), (
A.5
) and (
A.5
) we have:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\|\left({\bm{\epsilon}}+\mathbf{R}\right)^{\top}P_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}^{\perp}\mathbf{X}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}+K^{-2\upsilon}\asymp\lambda\,.

(A.6)

With this choice of
\lambda
, standard LASSO analysis completes the proof.
∎

Going back to the definition of
\hat{\omega}_{b}
(equation (
A.1
)), we expand the its estimation error as follows:

\displaystyle\hat{\omega}_{b}-\omega_{b}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\left(\frac{\mathbf{N}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{N}_{k}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\mathbf{N}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\left(\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0}\right)}{n}}_{T_{1}}+\underbrace{\left(\frac{\mathbf{N}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{N}_{k}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\mathbf{N}_{k}^{\top}(\mathbf{R}_{1}+\mathbf{R}_{2})}{n}}_{T_{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\underbrace{\left(\frac{\mathbf{N}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{N}_{k}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\mathbf{N}_{k}^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{n}}_{T_{3}}

We next bound each
T_{i}
separately. Same argument as for the fixed dimensional analysis (See the proof of Proposition
2.1
) establishes:

\left\|\left(\frac{\mathbf{N}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{N}_{k}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{op}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}1\,.

Therefore we can bound
T_{1}
as:

\displaystyle T_{1}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left\|\frac{\mathbf{N}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\left(\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0}\right)}{n}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{K}}{n}\max_{1\leq j\leq k}\left|\mathbf{N}_{K,*j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\left(\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{K}}{n}\left\|\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0}\right\|_{1}\times\max_{1\leq j\leq k}\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\left|\mathbf{N}_{K,*j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{*,l}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\sqrt{K}\left\|\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0}\right\|_{1}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\sqrt{K}s_{\beta}\left(\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}+K^{-2\upsilon}\right)\,.

For
T_{2}
and
T_{3}
, the term containing residuals:

\displaystyle T_{2}\leq\left(\frac{\mathbf{N}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{N}_{k}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\mathbf{N}_{k}^{\top}(\mathbf{R}_{1}+\mathbf{R}_{2})}{n}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(\frac{\mathbf{N}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{N}_{k}}{n}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|_{op}\left\|\frac{\mathbf{R}_{1}+\mathbf{R}_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left\|\frac{\mathbf{R}_{1}+\mathbf{R}_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{1}}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left(K^{-\upsilon}+\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}\right)\,.

where the last rate inequality follows from the bounds on
\mathbf{R}_{1}
and
\mathbf{R}_{2}
established in the proof of Lemma
A.2
. For the error term we use the sub-gaussian bound using the fact that
\mathbb{E}[{\epsilon}\mid X,Z,\eta]=0
. For this, note that for any vector
z\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
we have:

\|z\|=\sup_{\|v\|=1}|z^{\top}v|\,.

Define
\mathcal{N}_{1/2}(S^{K-1}
to be
1/2
-covering number of the sphere in dimension
K
. Then we know
\mathcal{N}_{1/2}(S^{K-1}\leq 5^{K}
. Also we have:

\|z\|\leq\sup_{v_{1}\in\mathcal{N}_{1/2}(S^{K-1})}|z^{\top}v_{1}|\leq\frac{1}{2}\|z\|

which implies:

\|z\|\leq 2\sup_{v_{1}\in\mathcal{N}_{1/2}(S^{K-1})}|z^{\top}v_{1}|\,.

Using this we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{v_{1}\in\mathcal{N}_{1/2}(S^{K-1})}\left|v_{1}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{N}_{K}^{\top}\mathbf{N}_{K}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{N}_{K}^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}\right|>t\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\eta}}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{v_{1}\in\mathcal{N}_{1/2}(S^{K-1})}\left|v_{1}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{N}_{K}^{\top}\mathbf{N}_{K}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{N}_{K}^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}\right|>t\mid\hat{\eta}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\eta}}\left[\sum_{v_{1}\in\mathcal{N}_{1/2}(S^{K-1})}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|v_{1}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{N}_{K}^{\top}\mathbf{N}_{K}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{N}_{K}^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}\right|>t\mid\hat{\eta}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\eta}}\left[\sum_{v_{1}\in\mathcal{N}_{1/2}(S^{K-1})}2{\sf exp}{\left(-c\frac{nt^{2}}{v_{1}^{\top}\left(\frac{\mathbf{N}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{N}_{k}}{n}\right)^{-1}v_{1}}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\eta}}\left[2{\sf exp}{\left(K\log{5}-c\frac{nt^{2}}{\left\|\left(\frac{\mathbf{N}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{N}_{k}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{op}}\right)}\right]

This implies:

T_{3}=\left(\frac{\mathbf{N}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{N}_{k}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\mathbf{N}_{k}^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{n}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\sqrt{\frac{K}{n}}\,.

Combining the bounds on
T_{1},T_{2},T_{3}
we have:

\left\|\hat{\omega}_{b}-\omega_{b}\right\|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\sqrt{K}s_{\beta}\left(\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}+K^{-2\upsilon}\right)+K^{-\upsilon}+\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{K}{n}}\,.

The above bound on the estimation error on
\omega_{b}
translates to the estimation error of
b^{\prime}
as follows:

\displaystyle\left|b^{\prime}(x)-\hat{b}^{\prime}(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\nabla N_{K}(x)\right\|\left\|\hat{\omega}_{b}-\omega_{b}\right\|+K^{-(\upsilon-1)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}K^{3/2}\left[\sqrt{K}s_{\beta}\left(\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}+K^{-2\upsilon}\right)+K^{-\upsilon}+\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{K}{n}}\right]+K^{-(\upsilon-1)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}K^{2}s_{\beta}\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}+K^{-(\upsilon-\frac{3}{2})}+K^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}+\frac{K^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}K^{2}s_{\beta}\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}+K^{-\left(\upsilon-\frac{3}{2}\right)}\,.

Hence an optimal choice of
K
would satisfy:

\displaystyle K^{2}s_{\beta}\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}\asymp K^{-\left(\upsilon-\frac{3}{2}\right)}\implies\

\displaystyle s_{\beta}\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}\asymp K^{-(\upsilon+\frac{1}{2})}

\displaystyle\implies\

\displaystyle\frac{s^{2}_{\beta}s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}\asymp K^{-(2\upsilon+1)}

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle K\asymp\left(\frac{n}{s^{2}_{\beta}s_{\gamma}\log{p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\upsilon+1}}

Using this we conclude:

\sup_{|t|\leq\tau}\left|b^{\prime}(t)-\hat{b}^{\prime}(t)\right|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{n}{s^{2}_{\beta}s_{\gamma}\log{p}}\right)^{\frac{-(\upsilon-\frac{3}{2})}{2\upsilon+1}}\,.

This completes the proof.
∎

A.3
Estimation of
\alpha_{0}
from
\mathcal{D}_{3}

For notational simplicity, we here use
r_{n}(=K^{-\upsilon})
to denote the B-spline approximation of
m_{j}
and
\check{m}_{j}
(see the definitions in Section
4
) and
\dot{r}_{n}
to denote the estimation error of
\hat{b}^{\prime}
obtained in Proposition
A.1
, i.e. we write:

1.

\sup_{|t|\leq\tau}\left|b^{\prime}(t)-\hat{b}^{\prime}(t)\right|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\dot{r}_{n}
.

2.

For
0\leq j\leq 1+p_{1}+p_{2}
,
\sup_{|t|\leq\tau}\left|m_{j}(t)-\mathbf{N}_{k}(t)^{\top}\omega_{j}\right|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}r_{n}
.

3.

For
0\leq j\leq 1+p_{1}+p_{2}
,
\sup_{|t|\leq\tau}\left|\check{m}_{j}(t)-\mathbf{N}_{k}(t)^{\top}\check{\omega}_{j}\right|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}r_{n}

where
\omega_{j}
and
\check{\omega}_{j}
are the optimal projection vectors of
m_{j}
and
\check{m}_{j}
respectively on the space spanned by
K
B-spline basis with respect to
\ell_{\infty}
norm. Henceforth, we will work on the intersection of these events.
First, Consider the LASSO regression of
\mathbf{S}^{\perp}
on
\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}^{\perp}
. By basic inequality we have:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n}\left\|\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right\|^{2}+\lambda_{1}\|\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\|_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2n}\left\|\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|^{2}+\lambda_{1}\|\theta^{*}_{S}\|_{1}

Few algebraic manipulations (similar to that of standard LASSO analysis) yields:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n}\left\|\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\left(\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}-\theta^{*}_{S}\right)\right\|^{2}+\lambda_{1}\|\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\leq\frac{1}{n}\left\|\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\theta^{*}_{S}\right)^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}-\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|_{1}+\lambda_{1}\|\theta^{*}_{S}\|_{1}

(A.7)

To find the optimal value of
\lambda_{1}
, we need to bound
(1/n)\|(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\theta^{*}_{S})^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\|_{\infty}
. Similarly, for the LASSO regression of
\mathbf{Y}
on
\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}
, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n}\left\|\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\left(\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}-\theta^{*}_{Y}\right)\right\|^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\leq\frac{1}{n}\left\|\left(\mathbf{Y}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\theta^{*}_{Y}\right)^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}-\theta^{*}_{Y}\right\|_{1}+\lambda_{0}\|\theta^{*}_{Y}\|_{1}

(A.8)

and to obtain
\lambda_{0}
, we need to bound
(1/n)\|(\mathbf{Y}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\theta^{*}_{Y})^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\|_{\infty}
. Towards that direction, we need the following lemma:

Lemma A.3
.

Define the random variable
\widehat{\tilde{W}}
(and consequently the matrix
\widehat{\tilde{\mathbf{W}}}
) and
W-\mathbb{E}[W\mid\hat{\eta}]
. Under our assumptions, we have for all
1\leq l\leq 1+p_{1}+p_{2}
we have with probability going to
1
:

\max_{1\leq j\leq 1+p_{1}+p_{2}}\frac{1}{n}\left\|\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{*,j}-\widehat{\tilde{\mathbf{W}}}_{*,j}\right\|^{2}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\ \dot{r}_{n}^{2}+\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}+\dot{r}_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}

\max_{1\leq j\leq 1+p_{1}+p_{2}}\frac{1}{n}\left\|\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{*,j}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j}\right\|^{2}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\ \dot{r}_{n}^{2}+\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}+\dot{r}_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}\,,

and

\max_{1\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq p}\frac{1}{n}\left|\left(\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j}^{\perp}\right)^{\top}\left(\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{*,j^{\prime}}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j^{\prime}}\right)\right|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\ \dot{r}_{n}+\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}\,.

The proof of this lemma can be found in the Supplementary document. An immediate consequence of Lemma
A.3
is the following bound which will be used subsequently in this proof:

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq j\neq j^{\prime}\leq p}\frac{1}{n}\left|\left(\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{*,j}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j}\right)^{\top}\left(\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{*,j^{\prime}}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j^{\prime}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\max_{1\leq j\leq p}\frac{1}{n}\left\|\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{*,j}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j}\right\|^{2}}\times\sqrt{\max_{1\leq j^{\prime}\leq p}\frac{1}{n}\left\|\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{*,j^{\prime}}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j^{\prime}}\right\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\ \dot{r}_{n}^{2}+\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}+\dot{r}_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}\,.

(A.9)

Based on the bound obtained in Lemma
A.3
, the optimal choices for
\lambda_{0}
and
\lambda_{1}
are following:

Lemma A.4
.

Under our assumptions, we can choose
\lambda_{0}
and
\lambda_{1}
as:

\lambda_{1}\asymp(1+\|\theta^{*}_{S}\|_{1})\left[\dot{r}_{n}+\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}\right]+\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}\left(\log{\frac{n}{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}}\right)^{3/2}\,,

\lambda_{0}\asymp(1+\|\theta^{*}_{Y}\|_{1})\left[\dot{r}_{n}+\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}\right]+\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}\left(\log{\frac{n}{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}}\right)^{3/2}\,.

where
\theta^{*}_{S},\theta^{*}_{Y}
are same as defined in Section
4
.

The proof of Lemma
A.4
is also presented in the Supplementary document. Another important ingredient in obtaining this LASSO-type bounds is the restricted eigenvalue assumption on the covariate matrix
\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}
which is presented in the next Proposition (proof is in Supplementary document):

Proposition A.5
.

Under our assumptions in Section
4
, the matrix
\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}
satisfies RE condition with high probability.

We next obtain the estimation error of
\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}
and
\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}
combining our findings from Lemma
A.4
and Proposition
A.5
. From (
A.3
) we have:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n}\left\|\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\left(\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}-\theta^{*}_{S}\right)\right\|^{2}+\lambda_{1}\|\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\|_{1}\leq\frac{\lambda_{1}}{2}\left\|\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}-\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|_{1}+\lambda_{1}\|\theta^{*}_{S}\|_{1}

Therefore by the choice of
\lambda_{1}
of Lemma
A.4
and Proposition
A.5
we have (via standard LASSO bound calculation):

\displaystyle\left\|\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}-\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|_{1}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\lambda_{1}s_{1}\,,

\displaystyle\left\|\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}-\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|^{2}_{2}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\lambda^{2}_{1}s_{1}\,.

Similarly, for
\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}
, we have from equation (
A.3
):

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n}\left\|\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\left(\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}-\theta^{*}_{Y}\right)\right\|^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}\|_{1}\leq\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2}\left\|\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}-\theta^{*}_{Y}\right\|_{1}+\lambda_{0}\|\theta^{*}_{Y}\|_{1}

As before, the value of
\lambda_{0}
from Lemma
A.4
and Proposition
A.5
yields:

\displaystyle\left\|\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}-\left(\theta^{*}_{S}\alpha_{0}+\theta^{*}_{0,-1}\right)\right\|_{1}\lesssim_{p}\lambda_{0}s_{0}\,,

\displaystyle\left\|\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}-\left(\theta^{*}_{S}\alpha_{0}+\theta^{*}_{0,-1}\right)\right\|^{2}_{2}\lesssim_{p}\lambda_{0}^{2}s_{0}\,.

From the above rates of the lasso estimates
\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}
and
\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}
we can further conclude:

\displaystyle\left\|\theta_{0,-1}+\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\alpha_{0}-\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\theta_{0,-1}+\theta^{*}_{S}\alpha_{0}-\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}\right\|_{1}+|\alpha_{0}|\left\|\theta^{*}_{S}-\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}|\alpha_{0}|\lambda_{1}s_{1}+\left\|\theta^{*}_{0,-1}+\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\alpha_{0}-\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}\right\|_{1}+\left\|\theta_{0,-1}-\theta^{*}_{0,-1}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\lambda_{0}s_{0}+|\alpha_{0}|\lambda_{1}s_{1}+s_{\gamma}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}

(A.10)

and similarly:

\displaystyle\left\|\theta^{0}_{-1}+\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\alpha_{0}-\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}\right\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{p}\lambda^{2}_{0}s_{0}+\alpha^{2}_{0}\lambda^{2}_{1}s_{1}+\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}\,.

(A.11)

Going back to equation (
4.9
), we next show in the following Proposition that the common denominator of the three terms in the RHS stabilizes:

Proposition A.6
.

Under our assumptions:

\frac{1}{n_{3}}\left\|\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right\|^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tilde{S}-\tilde{W}_{-1}^{\top}\theta^{*}_{S}\right)^{2}\mathds{1}_{|\eta|\leq\tau}\right]+o_{p}(1)

The proof of Proposition
A.6
can be found in the Appendix. We next show that the numerators of the first and the second term of the RHS of equation (
4.9
) are asymptotically negligible and the numerator of the third term contributes to asymptotic normality.

Numerator of first term:
We start with the first term, which is
\sqrt{n_{3}}
times:

\frac{1}{n_{3}}\left(\theta_{0,-1}+\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\alpha_{0}-\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}\right)^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right)

We first expand it as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left(\theta_{0,-1}+\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\alpha_{0}-\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}\right)^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\left(\theta_{0,-1}+\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\alpha_{0}-\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}\right)^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\theta^{*}_{S}\right)}_{T_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\left(\theta_{0,-1}+\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\alpha_{0}-\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}\right)^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}^{\perp^{\top}}\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}^{\perp}\left(\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}-\theta^{*}_{S}\right)}_{T_{2}}\,.

To bound
T_{1}
we use
\ell_{1}-\ell_{\infty}
bound:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left(\theta_{0,-1}+\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\alpha_{0}-\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}\right)^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\theta^{*}_{S}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\left\|\theta_{0,-1}+\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\alpha_{0}-\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}\right\|_{1}\times\frac{1}{n}\left\|\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\theta^{*}_{S}\right)\right\|_{\infty}

We have already established bound on the first part in equation (
A.10
) and the second part is bounded by
\lambda_{1}
(see the proof of Lemma
A.4
). Therefore we have with probability going to 1:

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\lambda_{1}\left(\lambda_{0}s_{0}+|\alpha_{0}|\lambda_{1}s_{1}+s_{\gamma}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\lambda_{0}\vee\lambda_{1}\right)^{2}(s_{0}\vee s_{1})+\lambda_{1}s_{\gamma}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}\,.

For
T_{2}
we can use CS inequality to conclude:

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\left\|\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}^{\perp}\left(\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}-\theta^{*}_{S}\right)\right\|^{2}}}_{T_{21}}\times\underbrace{\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\left\|\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}^{\perp}\left(\theta_{0,-1}+\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\alpha_{0}-\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}\right)\right\|^{2}}}_{T_{22}}

The first term
T_{21}
is the prediction error of Lasso when we regress
\mathbf{S}^{\perp}
on
\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{1}
. Therefore from standard Lasso prediction error bound we have:

T_{21}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\lambda_{1}\sqrt{s_{1}}\,,

For
T_{22}
we need a bit more detailed calculation. First of all note that,
T_{22}
can be further bounded as:

\displaystyle T^{2}_{22}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left\|\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}^{\perp}\left(\theta_{0,-1}+\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\alpha_{0}-\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}\right)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n}\left\|\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}^{\perp}\left(\theta_{0,-1}+\theta^{0}_{-1,1}\alpha_{0}-\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}\right)\right\|^{2}+\frac{\alpha_{0}^{2}}{n}\left\|\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}^{\perp}\left(\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}-\theta^{0}_{-1,1}\right)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\frac{1}{n}\left\|\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}^{\perp}\left(\theta^{*}_{0,-1}+\theta^{0}_{-1,1}\alpha_{0}-\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}\right)\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\left\|\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}^{\perp}\left(\theta^{*}_{0,-1}-\theta^{0}_{-1}\right)\right\|^{2}+\lambda_{1}^{2}s_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\lambda_{0}^{2}s_{0}+\lambda_{1}^{2}s_{1}+\frac{1}{n}\left\|P_{N_{k}}^{\perp}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}\left(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\right)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\lambda_{0}^{2}s_{0}+\lambda_{1}^{2}s_{1}+\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}\times\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\tilde{Z}_{i}^{\top}a_{n}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{0}^{2}s_{0}+\lambda_{1}^{2}s_{1}+\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}\times\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\hat{b}^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i})\right)^{2}\left(Z_{i}^{\top}a_{n}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{0}^{2}s_{0}+\lambda_{1}^{2}s_{1}+\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}\times\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(b^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i})\right)^{2}\left(Z_{i}^{\top}a_{n}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\hat{b}^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i})-b^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i})\right)^{2}\left(Z_{i}^{\top}a_{n}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\lambda_{0}^{2}s_{0}+\lambda_{1}^{2}s_{1}+\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}\times\left[1+\dot{r}_{n}\right]\,.

where in the last inequality, we use the following facts:

\displaystyle b^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i})

\displaystyle\lesssim 1\,,\hskip 14.45377pt[\text{As }b^{\prime}\text{ is bounded, Assumption \ref{assm:smooth_b}}]

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(b^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i})\right)^{2}\left(Z_{i}^{\top}a_{n}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}1\,,\hskip 14.45377pt[b^{\prime}\text{ is bounded and Lemma \ref{lem:subg_second_moment}}]

\displaystyle\left\|\hat{b}^{\prime}-b^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}r^{\prime}_{n}\hskip 14.45377pt[\text{Proposition }\ref{prop:b_hat_est}]\,.

Therefore we have:

T_{22}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\lambda_{0}\sqrt{s_{0}}+\lambda_{1}\sqrt{s_{1}}+\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}\,.

Taking products of the bounds on
T_{21}
and
T_{22}
we have:

\displaystyle T_{2}\leq T_{21}\times T_{22}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\lambda_{1}\sqrt{s_{1}}\left(\lambda_{0}\sqrt{s_{0}}+\lambda_{1}\sqrt{s_{1}}+\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\lambda_{0}\vee\lambda_{1}\right)^{2}(s_{0}\vee s_{1})+\lambda_{1}\sqrt{\frac{s_{1}s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}

Combining bounds on
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
we obtain that for the second term in equation (
4.9
), with probability going to 1:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left(\theta^{0}_{-1}+\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\alpha_{0}-\hat{\theta}_{-1,Y}\right)^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\lambda_{0}\vee\lambda_{1}\right)^{2}(s_{0}\vee s_{1})+\lambda_{1}s_{\gamma}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}+\left(\lambda_{0}\vee\lambda_{1}\right)^{2}(s_{0}\vee s_{1})+\lambda_{1}\sqrt{\frac{s_{1}s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\lambda_{0}\vee\lambda_{1}\right)^{2}(s_{0}\vee s_{1})+\lambda_{1}\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}(s_{1}+s_{\gamma})\log{p}}{n}}

which implies:

\textbf{Numerator of Term 1 }\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\sqrt{n}\left[\left(\lambda_{0}\vee\lambda_{1}\right)^{2}(s_{0}\vee s_{1})+\lambda_{1}\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}(s_{1}+s_{\gamma})\log{p}}{n}}\right]\,.

(A.12)

For the above term to be asymptotically negligible we need:

\sqrt{n}\left[\left(\lambda_{0}\vee\lambda_{1}\right)^{2}(s_{0}\vee s_{1})+\lambda_{1}\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}(s_{1}+s_{\gamma})\log{p}}{n}}\right]=o(1)\,.

Numerator of the second term:
We next control
\sqrt{n}
times the numerator of second term on the RHS of equation (
4.9
) which involves the remainder
\tilde{\mathbf{R}}
. Recall that
\tilde{\mathbf{R}}=\mathbf{R}_{1}+\mathbf{R}_{2}+\mathbf{R}_{3}
where:

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{1}

\displaystyle=b(\hat{\eta})-\mathbf{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})\omega_{b}

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{2}

\displaystyle=\left(b^{\prime}(\hat{\eta})-\hat{b}^{\prime}(\hat{\eta})\right)\odot Z(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0})

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{3}

\displaystyle=(\eta-\hat{\eta})^{2}\odot b^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\eta})

Therefore the numerator of the second term can be written as:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\tilde{\mathbf{R}}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right)}_{T_{1}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{2}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right)}_{T_{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{3}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right)}_{T_{3}}\,.

To bound
T_{1}
note that
\mathbf{R}_{1}
is measurable function of
\hat{\eta}
. We further expand
T_{1}
as:

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\hat{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}}^{\perp}-\hat{\tilde{\mathbf{W}}}^{\perp}_{-1}\theta^{*}_{S}\right)}_{T_{11}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\hat{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}}\right)}_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}-\hat{\tilde{\mathbf{W}}}_{-1}\right)\theta^{*}_{S}}_{T_{13}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\perp^{\top}}\hat{\tilde{\mathbf{W}}}_{-1}\left(\theta^{*}_{S}-\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right)}_{T_{14}}

Note that given
\hat{\eta}
,
T_{11}
is a linear combination of centered subgaussian random variables. Therefore by subgaussian concentration inequality, we have with probabilities going to 1:

T_{11}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\frac{r_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\,.

Further we have as
\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\hat{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}}=P_{\mathbf{N}_{k}}^{\perp}\check{\mathbf{R}}_{1}-P_{\mathbf{N}_{k}}\check{\nu}_{1}
we have with probability going to:

T_{12}=\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\perp^{\top}}\check{\mathbf{R}}_{1}\leq r_{n}^{2}\,.

For
T_{13}
we apply a similar analysis along with
\ell_{1}-\ell_{\infty}
bound:

\displaystyle T_{13}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|_{1}\frac{1}{n}\left\|\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}-\hat{\tilde{\mathbf{W}}}_{-1}\right)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\left\|\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|_{1}\max_{2\leq j\leq 2p}\frac{1}{n}\left|\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j}^{\perp}-\hat{\tilde{\mathbf{W}}}_{*,j}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|_{1}\max_{2\leq j\leq 2p}\frac{1}{n}\left[\left|\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j}^{\perp}-\mathbf{W}_{*,j}^{\perp}\right)\right|+\left|\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\mathbf{W}_{*,j}^{\perp}-\hat{\tilde{\mathbf{W}}}_{*,j}\right)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|_{1}\max_{p+1\leq j\leq 2p}\frac{1}{n}\left|\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j}^{\perp}-\mathbf{W}_{*,j}^{\perp}\right)\right|+\left\|\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|_{1}\max_{2\leq j\leq 2p}\frac{1}{n}\left|\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\perp^{\top}}\check{\mathbf{R}}_{j}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|_{1}\max_{p+1\leq j\leq 2p}\sqrt{\frac{\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\perp^{\top}}\mathbf{R}_{1}}{n}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}Z_{i,j}^{2}\left(\hat{b}(\hat{\eta}_{i})-b(\eta_{i})\right)^{2}}+\left\|\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|_{1}r_{n}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left\|\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|_{1}\left[r_{n}\left(\dot{r}_{n}+\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}\right)\right]+\left\|\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|_{1}r_{n}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left\|\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|_{1}\left[r_{n}\left(\dot{r}_{n}+\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}\right)\right]\,.

And for the last term
T_{14}
we have:

\displaystyle T_{14}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\perp^{\top}}\hat{\tilde{\mathbf{W}}}_{-1}\left(\theta^{*}_{S}-\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\theta^{*}_{S}-\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right\|_{1}\max_{1\leq j\leq p}\frac{1}{n}\left|\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\perp^{\top}}\hat{\tilde{\mathbf{W}}}_{*,j}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{1}s_{1}r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}\,.

Combining the bounds on the different components of
T_{1}
we have with probability going to 1:

\displaystyle T_{1}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\frac{r_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}+r_{n}^{2}+\left\|\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|_{1}\left[r_{n}\left(\dot{r}_{n}+\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}\right)\right]+\lambda_{1}s_{1}r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}\,.

Now we consider the second remainder term
T_{2}
which involves
\mathbf{R}_{2}
. An easy bound on this term will be following:

\displaystyle T_{2}=\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{2}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\left\|\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right\|^{2}}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\dot{r}_{n}\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}\,.

Finally, for the third term
T_{3}
an easy CS bound is sufficient:

T_{3}=\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{3}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right)\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{R}_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{3}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\left\|\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right\|^{2}}\lesssim\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}\,.

Combining the bounds on
T_{1},T_{2},T_{3}
we have with probability going to 1:

\displaystyle T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\frac{r_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}+r_{n}^{2}+\left\|\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|_{1}\left[r_{n}\left(\dot{r}_{n}+\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}\right)\right]+\lambda_{1}s_{1}r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\dot{r}_{n}\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}+\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}\,.

which implies:

Numerator 2nd

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\sqrt{n}\left[\frac{r_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}+r_{n}^{2}+\left\|\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|_{1}\left[r_{n}\left(\dot{r}_{n}+\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}\right)\right]+\lambda_{1}s_{1}r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.+\dot{r}_{n}\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}+\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}\right]\,.

(A.13)

Numerator of the third term:
Last but not the least, we need to analyze the numerator of the error term, i.e. third term on the RHS of equation (
4.9
) First we show that:

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{S}}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}\theta^{*}_{S}\right)+o_{p}(1)\,.

That the first term on the RHS is
O_{p}(1)
is from the sub-exponential concentration inequality which will later be shown to be asymptotically normal. To show that the remainder is asymptotically negligible, first observe that:

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\perp^{\top}}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right)

which follows from the idempotence of the projection matrix. So it is enough to show that:

\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\tilde{\mathbf{S}}\right)}_{T_{1}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\right)\theta^{*}_{S}}_{T_{2}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}^{\perp}\left(\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}-\theta^{*}_{S}\right)}_{T_{3}}=o_{p}(1)\,.

To bound
T_{1}
we use the subgaussian concentration inequality along with the fact that
\mathbb{E}[{\epsilon}\mid X,Z,\eta]=0
. Therefore the terms of
{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\tilde{\mathbf{S}}\right)
are centered subgaussian random variables conditional on
(X,Z,\eta)
. So we have:

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left|{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\tilde{\mathbf{S}}\right)\right|>t\mid\sigma\left(X,Z,\eta,\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2}\right)\right)\leq 2{\sf exp}{\left(-c\frac{t^{2}}{\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}\frac{\left\|\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\tilde{\mathbf{S}}\right\|^{2}}{n}}\right)}

As we have already established in Lemma
A.3
that
\|\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\tilde{\mathbf{S}}\|^{2}/n=o_{p}(1)
, by DCT we conclude that
T_{1}=o_{p}(1)
. Similar subgaussian concentration for
T_{2}
yields:

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left|{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\right)\theta^{*}_{S}\right|>t\mid\sigma\left(X,Z,\eta\right)\right)\leq 2{\sf exp}{\left(-c\frac{t^{2}}{\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}\frac{\left\|\left(\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\right)\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|^{2}}{n}}\right)}

We have established in the proof of Proposition
A.6
that
\left\|\left(\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}-\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{-1}\right)\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|^{2}/n=o_{p}(1)
. Therefore, again by DCT we have
T_{2}=o_{p}(1)
.

Finally for
T_{3}
, we first use
\ell_{1}-\ell_{\infty}
bound:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}^{\perp}\left(\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}-\theta^{*}_{S}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}-\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|_{1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}^{\perp}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\left\|\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}-\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|_{1}\max_{1\leq j\leq p}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left|{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j}^{\perp}\right|

(A.14)

The rest of the proof is purely technical. Recall the definition of
\tilde{W}_{j}=W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}\mid\eta]
. Now in our matrix

\displaystyle\Sigma

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\eta}\left[\left(W-\mathbb{E}[W\mid\eta]\right)\left(W-\mathbb{E}[W\mid\eta]\right)^{\top}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\eta}\left[{\sf var}(W\mid\eta)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\eta}\left[{\sf var}\left(\begin{pmatrix}S&X&b^{\prime}(\eta)Z\end{pmatrix}\mid\eta\right)\right]\,.

As per our Assumption
4.6
the eigenvalues of this matrix are bounded away from 0 and infinity. Therefore any diagonal entries are bounded in between
(C_{\min},C_{\max})
(the notations in Assumption
4.6
). So from the sub-exponential Bernstein’s inequality we have for any constant
\upsilon>0
:

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{i,j}^{2}>(\upsilon+1)\Sigma_{j,j}\right)\leq 2{\sf exp}{\left(-c\min\left\{\frac{n\upsilon^{2}\Sigma_{j,j}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}_{W}},\frac{n\upsilon\Sigma_{j,j}}{\sigma_{W}}\right\}\right)}

If we define the above event
\mathcal{A}_{j}=\left\{(1/n)\sum_{i}\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{i,j}^{2}>(\upsilon+1)\Sigma_{j,j}\right\}
then we have:

\mathbb{P}\left(\cup_{j=1}^{p}\mathcal{A}_{j}\right)\leq 2{\sf exp}{\left(\log{p}-c\min\left\{\frac{n\upsilon^{2}C_{\max}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}_{W}},\frac{n\upsilon C_{\max}}{\sigma_{W}}\right\}\right)}

and the complement event is:

\mathbb{A}_{n,p}=\cap_{j}\mathcal{A}_{j}^{c}=\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{i,j}^{2}\leq(\upsilon+1)\Sigma_{j,j}\ \ \forall\ \ 1\leq j\leq p\right\}

Furthermore we have proved in the second part of Lemma
A.3
that with probability going to 1:

\max_{1\leq j\leq p}\frac{1}{n}\left\|\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}_{*,j}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j}\right\|^{2}\lesssim\left[r_{n}^{2}+\left(\frac{\log{p}}{n}\vee r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}\right)\right]\vee\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}\,.

Call that above event
\mathbb{B}_{n,p}
. On the event
\mathbb{A}_{n,p}\cap\mathbb{B}_{n,p}
, we have:

\frac{1}{n}\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j}^{\perp^{\top}}\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j}^{\perp}\lesssim\left[r_{n}^{2}+\left(\frac{\log{p}}{n}\vee r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}\right)\right]\vee\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}+(\upsilon+1)C_{\max}\lesssim(\upsilon+1)C_{\max}\,.

Using the above findings we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left|{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j}^{\perp}\right|>t\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left|{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j}^{\perp}\right|>t,\left(\mathbb{A}_{n,p}\cap\mathbb{B}_{n,p}\right)\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left(\mathbb{A}_{n,p}\cap\mathbb{B}_{n,p}\right)^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left|{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j}^{\perp}\right|>t\mid\sigma(X,Z,\eta)\right)\mathds{1}_{\left(\mathbb{A}_{n,p}\cap\mathbb{B}_{n,p}\right)}\right]+\mathbb{P}\left(\left(\mathbb{A}_{n,p}\cap\mathbb{B}_{n,p}\right)^{c}\right)

\displaystyle=2\sum_{j}\mathbb{E}\left[{\sf exp}{\left(-c\frac{t^{2}}{\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}\frac{\left(\mathbf{W}_{*,j}^{\perp}\right)^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{*,j}^{\perp}}{n}}\right)}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}_{n,p}\cap\mathbb{B}_{n,p}}\right]+\mathbb{P}\left(\left(\mathbb{A}_{n,p}\cap\mathbb{B}_{n,p}\right)^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2{\sf exp}{\left(\log{p}-c\frac{t^{2}}{(\upsilon+1)C_{\max}}\right)}+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left(\left(\mathbb{A}_{n,p}\cap\mathbb{B}_{n,p}\right)^{c}\right)}_{\to 0\text{ as we proved }}

Therefore we conclude that:

\max_{1\leq j\leq p}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left|{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j}^{\perp}\right|\lesssim_{p}\sqrt{\log{p}}

which, along with equation (
A.14
) concludes with probability going to 1:

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}^{\perp}\left(\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}-\theta^{*}_{S}\right)\lesssim\lambda_{1}s_{1}\sqrt{\log{p}}=o_{p}(1)\,.

We next show that:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{S}}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}\theta^{*}_{S}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\epsilon}_{i}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{i}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{i,-1}^{\top}\theta^{*}_{S}\right)\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\epsilon}_{i}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{i}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{i,-1}^{\top}\theta^{*}_{S}\right)\mathds{1}_{|\eta_{i}|\leq\tau}+o_{p}(1)\,.

which we will achieve by simple Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. Note that conditional on
X,Z,\eta,\mathcal{D}_{1}
the term
{\epsilon}_{i}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{i}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{i,-1}^{\top}\theta^{*}_{S}\right)\left(\mathds{1}_{|\eta_{i}|\leq\tau}-\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau}\right)
is a centered sub-gaussian random variable, where the sub-gaussianity follows from the sub-gaussianity of
{\epsilon}
. Therefore we need to show:

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{i}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{i,-1}^{\top}\theta^{*}_{S}\right)^{2}\left(\mathds{1}_{|\eta_{i}|\leq\tau}-\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau}\right)^{2}=o_{p}(1)\,.

Consequently it is enough to show:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tilde{S}-\tilde{W}_{-1}^{\top}\theta^{*}_{S}\right)^{2}\left(\mathds{1}_{|\eta|\leq\tau}-\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}|\leq\tau}\right)^{2}\mid\mathcal{D}_{1}\right]=o_{p}(1)\,.

From Holder inequality:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tilde{S}-\tilde{W}_{-1}^{\top}\theta^{*}_{S}\right)^{2}\left(\mathds{1}_{|\eta|\leq\tau}-\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}|\leq\tau}\right)^{2}\mid\mathcal{D}_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tilde{S}-\tilde{W}_{-1}^{\top}\theta^{*}_{S}\right)^{2+\delta}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\frac{\delta}{2}}}\times\left(\mathbb{P}(\Delta_{n}\mid\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)^{\frac{\frac{\delta}{2}}{1+\frac{\delta}{2}}}

where
\Delta_{n}
is the event that at-least one of the random variable
\eta
or
\hat{\eta}
is outside the interval
[-\tau,\tau]
. The first term in the above inequality is finite by Assumption
4.6
and therefore it is enough to show
\mathbb{P}(\Delta_{n}\mid\mathcal{D}_{1})=o_{p}(1)
which follows from equation (
H.12
) and (
H.13
) in the proof of Lemma
A.4
presented in the supplementary document.

The final part of the proof is to establish asymptotic normality, which again follows from simple application of Lindeberg’s central limit theorem along with Lyapounov’s condition. Define a set of triangular array of random variables
\{\zeta_{n,i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
as:

\zeta_{n,i}=\frac{{\epsilon}_{i}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{i}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{i,-1}^{\top}\theta^{*}_{S}\right)\mathds{1}_{|\eta_{i}|\leq\tau}}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{n,1}}

with
\sigma_{n,1}
being the standard deviation defined as:

\sigma_{n,1}=\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[{\epsilon}^{2}\left(\tilde{S}-\tilde{W}_{-1}^{\top}\theta^{*}_{S}\right)^{2}\left(\mathds{1}_{|\eta|\leq\tau}\right)\right]}\,.

Therefore,
\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{n,i}]=0
and
\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[\zeta^{2}_{n,i}]=1
. Furthermore from Assumption 1, we have:

\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[\left|\zeta_{n,i}\right|^{2+\delta}]=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left|{\epsilon}\left(\tilde{S}-\tilde{W}_{-1}^{\top}\theta^{*}_{S}\right)\right|^{2+\delta}\left(\mathds{1}_{|\eta|\leq\tau}\right)\right]}{n^{\delta}\sigma_{n,1}^{2+\delta}}\to 0\,.

Hence we conclude:

\frac{1}{\sigma_{n}\sqrt{n}}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{S}^{\perp}-\check{\mathbf{W}}_{-1}\hat{\theta}_{-1,S}\right)\overset{\mathscr{L}}{\implies}\mathcal{N}(0,1)\,.

∎

A.4
Some sufficient conditions for normality

In the proof of Theorem
4.10
, we need to ensure that RHS of equation (
A.12
) and (
A.13
) are
o(1)
to ensure asymptotic normality of the debiased estimator. We here present some sufficient conditions in certain cases:

A.4.1
Case 1:

Assume
s_{0}=s_{1}=s_{\gamma}=s_{\beta}\sim s
, i.e. all the sparsities are of similar order and
\|\theta^{*}_{S}\|_{1}\sim\|\theta^{*}_{Y}\|_{1}\sim\sqrt{s}
. Further assume that
\dot{r}_{n}\gg\sqrt{(s\log{p}/n)}
. We start with (
A.12
) which requires:

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left[\left(\lambda_{0}\vee\lambda_{1}\right)^{2}(s_{0}\vee s_{1})+\lambda_{1}\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}(s_{1}+s_{\gamma})\log{p}}{n}}\right]=o(1)

(A.15)

From Lemma
A.4
we have under our setup:

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\sim\lambda_{0}\sim\sqrt{s}\left[\dot{r}_{n}+\sqrt{\frac{s\log{p}}{n}}\right]+\sqrt{\frac{s\log{p}}{n}}\left(\log{\frac{n}{s\log{p}}}\right)^{3/2}\,.

Ignoring the log factor we have:

\lambda_{1}\sim\lambda_{0}\sim\sqrt{s}\dot{r}_{n}+\sqrt{\frac{s\log{p}}{n}}\,.

Therefore the condition in equation (
A.15
) simplifies to:

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left(\lambda_{0}\vee\lambda_{1}\right)^{2}(s_{0}\vee s_{1})

\displaystyle\sim\sqrt{n}s\left(s\dot{r}_{n}^{2}+\frac{s\log{p}}{n}\right)

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\lambda_{1}\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}(s_{1}+s_{\gamma})\log{p}}{n}}

\displaystyle\sim\sqrt{s\log{p}}\left(\sqrt{s}\dot{r}_{n}+\sqrt{\frac{s\log{p}}{n}}\right)\,.

For both the terms on the RHS of the above equations to be
o(1)
we need:

\displaystyle\dot{r}_{n}

\displaystyle=o\left(\frac{1}{sn^{1/4}\wedge\frac{1}{s\sqrt{\log{p}}}}\right)\,,

(A.16)

\displaystyle\frac{s\log{p}}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle=o(1)\,.

(A.17)

Now for equation (
A.13
) to be asymptotically negligible, we need:

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left[\frac{r_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}+r_{n}^{2}+\left\|\theta^{*}_{S}\right\|_{1}\left[r_{n}\left(\dot{r}_{n}+\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}\right)\right]+\lambda_{1}s_{1}r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.+\dot{r}_{n}\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}+\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}\right]=o(1)\,.

(A.18)

which in our above setup, reduces to:

\displaystyle r_{n}+\sqrt{n}r_{n}^{2}+\sqrt{ns}r_{n}\dot{r}_{n}+sr_{n}\sqrt{\log{p}}+\dot{r}_{n}\sqrt{s\log{p}}+s^{3/2}\dot{r}_{n}r_{n}\sqrt{\log{p}}+\frac{s^{3/2}r_{n}\log{p}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{s\log{p}}{\sqrt{n}}=o(1)\,.

As described at the beginning of subsection
A.3
, in most of the scenarios, we expect
r_{n}\ll\dot{r}_{n}
. Condition (
A.16
) implies that first seven summands of the above equation are o(1), whereas (
A.17
) implies the last summand is o(1). Finally, for the proof of Proposition
A.6
,
A.5
and Lemma
I.3
we further need that:

(s_{0}\vee s_{1})\left\{\dot{r}_{n}^{2}+\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}+\dot{r}_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}\right\}=o(1)\,.

(A.19)

which again holds under (
A.16
) and (
A.17
). Therefore, in this setup, (
A.16
) and (
A.17
) are sufficient to ensure asymptotic normality.

A.4.2
Case 2:

Now assume that
\dot{r}_{n}\ll\sqrt{(s\log{p})/n}
whereas the other conditions remain same, i.e.
s_{0}=s_{1}=s_{\gamma}=s_{\beta}\sim s
and
\|\theta^{*}_{S}\|_{1}\sim\|\theta^{*}_{Y}\|_{1}\sim\sqrt{s}
. In this case the order of
\lambda_{0}
and
\lambda_{1}
becomes (ignoring the log factor):

\lambda_{0}\sim\lambda_{1}\sim s\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}\,.

Consequently, the condition of (
A.15
) simplifies to:

\sqrt{n}\left[\frac{s^{2}\log{p}}{n}+\frac{s^{3/2}\log{p}}{n}\right]=o(1)\,.

A sufficient condition for this is:

\frac{s^{2}\log{p}}{\sqrt{n}}=o(1)\,.

(A.20)

In this setup, the bound in equation (
A.18
) reduces to:

r_{n}+\sqrt{n}r_{n}^{2}+s\sqrt{n}r_{n}\dot{r}_{n}+sr_{n}\sqrt{\log{p}}+\frac{s^{2}r_{n}\log{p}}{\sqrt{n}}+\dot{r}_{n}\sqrt{s\log{p}}+\frac{s\log{p}}{\sqrt{n}}\,.

From the conditions
\dot{r}_{n}\ll\sqrt{(s\log{p})/n}
and
r_{n}\ll\dot{r}_{n}
and condition (
A.20
) it is immediate that the bound in the above display is
o(1)
. Furthermore, for the proof of Proposition
A.5
,
A.6
and Lemma
I.3
we need to establish equation (
A.19
), which is also immediate from condition
A.20
. Therefore, condition
A.20
is sufficient to ensure asymptotic normality of the debiased estimator.

Appendix B
Proof of Theorem
3.6

B.1
Proof of Step 1

First we decompose the matrix as follows:

\frac{\mathbf{W}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K,a}}\mathbf{W}}{n}=\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{W}_{1}}{n}+\frac{\mathbf{W}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{2}}{n}

We show that:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{W}_{1}}{n}

\displaystyle\overset{P}{\to}\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{E}\left[{\sf var}\left(\begin{bmatrix}s&\mathbf{x}&\mathbf{z}b^{\prime}(\eta)\end{bmatrix}\,\middle|\,\eta\right)\mathds{1}_{|\eta|\leq\tau}\right]\,,

(B.1)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{W}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{2}}{n}

\displaystyle\overset{P}{\to}\frac{1}{3}\begin{bmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
0&0&\Sigma_{Z}\end{bmatrix}

(B.2)

where
\mathbf{W}_{1},\mathbf{W}_{2}
are as defined in Section
2
. Note that this implies:

\frac{\mathbf{W}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K,a}}\mathbf{W}}{n}\overset{P}{\to}\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{E}\left[{\sf var}\left(\begin{bmatrix}s&\mathbf{x}&\mathbf{z}b^{\prime}(\eta)\end{bmatrix}\,\middle|\,\eta\right)\mathds{1}_{|\eta|\leq\tau}\right]+\frac{1}{3}\begin{bmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
0&0&\Sigma_{Z}\end{bmatrix}=\frac{1}{3}\Omega_{\tau}

which delivers the assertion of Step 1.

Equation (
B.2
) follows immediately from an application of weak law of large numbers. For equation (
B.1
), note that
\mathbf{W}_{1}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{W}_{1}/n
is the norm of the residual of rows of
\mathbf{W}_{1}
upon projecting out the effect of
\mathbf{N}_{K}
, which can be further decomposed as:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{W}_{1}}{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*^{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}}{n}+2\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*^{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}(\mathbf{W}_{1}-\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1})}{n}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{(\mathbf{W}_{1}-\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1})^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}(\mathbf{W}_{1}-\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1})}{n}

(B.3)

where
\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}=\begin{bmatrix}S_{i}&X_{i}&b^{\prime}(\eta_{i})Z_{i}\end{bmatrix}
. Note that the only difference between
\mathbf{W}_{1}
and
\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}
is in the last
p_{2}
co-ordinates where we have replaced
\hat{b}^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i})
by
b^{\prime}(\eta_{i})
. We now show that:

\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*^{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*}-\mathbf{W}_{1})}{n}\overset{P}{\longrightarrow}0\,.

The other term
(\mathbf{W}_{1}-\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1})^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}(\mathbf{W}_{1}-\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1})/n
will consequently be
o_{p}(1)
as it is a lower order term Fix
1\leq j,k\leq 1+p_{1}+p_{2}
:

\displaystyle\left|\left(\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*^{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*}-\mathbf{W}_{1})}{n}\right)_{j,k}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{\left\langle{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,*j},{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{W}_{1})_{*k}\right\rangle}{n}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{\|\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,*j}\|^{2}}{n}\frac{\|(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{W}_{1})_{*k}\|^{2}}{n}}

That
\|\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,*j}\|^{2}/n=O_{p}(1)
follows from an immediate application of WLLN. To show that the other part is
o_{p}(1)
, note that
\|(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{W}_{1})_{*k}\|^{2}/n=0
for
1\leq k\leq p_{1}+1
. For
p_{1}+2\leq k\leq p_{1}+p_{2}
, define
\tilde{k}=k-(p_{1}+1)
. Then:

\displaystyle\frac{\|(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{W}_{1})_{*k}\|^{2}}{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\left(b^{\prime}(\eta_{i})-\hat{b}^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i})\right)^{2}Z_{i,\tilde{k}}^{2}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\left(b^{\prime}(\eta_{i})-b^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i})\right)^{2}Z_{i,\tilde{k}}^{2}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(b^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i})-\hat{b}^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i})\right)^{2}Z_{i,\tilde{k}}^{2}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left[\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\left(\eta_{i}-\hat{\eta}_{i}\right)^{2}(b^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\eta}_{i}))^{2}Z_{i,\tilde{k}}^{2}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau}+\sup_{|x|\leq\tau}|b^{\prime}(t)-\hat{b}^{\prime}(t)|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z^{2}_{i,\tilde{k}}\right]

\displaystyle=O_{p}(n^{-1})+O_{p}(n^{-2})+o_{p}(1)=o_{p}(1)\,.

(B.4)

That the first summand is
O_{p}(n^{-1})
follows from the fact
b^{\prime\prime}
is uniformly bounded (Assumption
3.2
) and
\hat{\eta}_{i}-\eta_{i}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
and the second summand is
o_{p}(1)
follows from Proposition
2.1
. We next show:

\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*^{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}}{n}\overset{P}{\longrightarrow}\frac{1}{3}\Omega_{\tau}\,.

(B.5)

Towards that direction, we first claim that
(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*^{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}/n)=O_{p}(1)
. For any
1\leq l,m\leq 1+p_{1}+p_{2}
, we have:

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*^{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}}{n}\right|_{l,m}

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{\langle\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,*l},{\sf proj}_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,*m}\rangle}{n}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,*l}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\left\|\frac{\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,*m}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|=O_{p}(1)

by WLLN. Setting
a_{n}=\log{n}/\sqrt{n}
, we next decompose this term into two further terms:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*^{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}}{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*^{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}}{n}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}+\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*^{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}}{n}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|>a_{n}}

As we have already established
(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*^{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}/n)=O_{p}(1)
, it is immediate that:

\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*^{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}}{n}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|>a_{n}}=o_{p}(1)\,.

Therefore, we need to establish the convergence of
(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*^{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1})/n\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}
. Define a function
g(a,t)
and
V(a,t)
as:

\displaystyle g(a,t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\begin{pmatrix}S&X&b^{\prime}(\eta)Z\end{pmatrix}\,\middle|\,\eta+a^{\top}Z=t\right]\,,

\displaystyle V(a,t)

\displaystyle={\sf var}\left[\begin{pmatrix}S&X&b^{\prime}(\eta)Z\end{pmatrix}\,\middle|\,\eta+a^{\top}Z=t\right]

The following lemma characterizes some smoothness properties of the functions
g,V
:

Lemma B.1
.

Under Assumptions
3.1
-
3.4
, the functions
g
and
V
are continuous. Moreover
g
is continuously differentiable in both of its co-ordinates and consequently
g,\partial_{a}g
and
\partial_{t}g
are uniformly bounded on
\|a\|\leq 1
and
|t|\leq\tau+1
.

The proof of this lemma can be found in Section
E
. Note that the definition of
g
implies
g(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},t)=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i}\,\middle|\,\hat{\eta}=t,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right]
. As
g
is a vector valued functions with range being a subset of
\mathbb{R}^{1+p_{1}+p_{2}}
, we henceforth denote by
g_{j}
, the
j^{th}
co-ordinate of
g
for
1\leq j\leq 1+p_{1}+p_{2}
. Now for each of the co-ordinates of
g
, we further define
\omega_{j,n,\infty}
as the B-spline approximation vector of
g_{j}
, i.e.

\omega_{j,n,\infty}=\mathop{\rm argmin}_{\omega}\sup_{|x|\leq\tau}\left|g_{j}(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},x)-\tilde{N}_{K}(x)^{\top}\omega\right|

where
\tilde{N}_{k}
is the scaled B-spline basis functions (for definition and brief discussion, see Section
F
). We often drop the index
n
from
\omega_{j,n,\infty}
when there is no disambiguity. It is immediate from Theorem
F.1
(with
l=r=0
):

\sup_{|x|\leq\tau}\left|g_{j}(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},x)-\tilde{N}_{K}(x)^{\top}\omega_{j,\infty}\right|\lesssim\frac{2\tau}{K}\sup_{|x|\leq\tau}\left|\partial_{t}g(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},t)\right|=O_{p}(K^{-1})\,.

(B.6)

We define the matrix
\mathbf{G}
as
\mathbf{G}_{i*}=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}\,\middle|\,\hat{\eta}=\hat{\eta}_{i},\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right]=g(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},\hat{\eta}_{i})
and the matrix
\mathbf{H}
as
\mathbf{H}_{i,j}=\tilde{N}_{K}(\hat{\eta}_{i})^{\top}\omega_{j,\infty}
. Using these matrices we expand the matrix under consideration as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*^{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}}{n}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{G})^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{G})}{n}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{H})^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{H})}{n}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2\frac{(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{H})^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{G})}{n}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{G})^{\top}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{G})}{n}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}-\frac{(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{G})^{\top}{\sf proj}_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{G})}{n}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{H})^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{H})}{n}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2\frac{(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{H})^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{G})}{n}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle:=T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4}

(B.7)

We first show that
[(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*}-\mathbf{G})^{\top}(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*}-\mathbf{G})/n]\mathds{1}(\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n})
converges to some matrix. Towards that end, we further expand it as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*}-\mathbf{G})^{\top}(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*}-\mathbf{G})}{n}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},\hat{\eta}_{i}))(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},\hat{\eta}_{i}))^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau,\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i}))(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i}))^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau,\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i}))(g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i})-g(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},\hat{\eta}_{i}))^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau,\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}(g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i})-g(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},\hat{\eta}_{i}))(g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i})-g(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},\hat{\eta}_{i}))^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau,\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle:=T_{11}+T_{12}+T_{13}

We now show that
T_{12}=o_{p}(1)
and
T_{13}=o_{p}(1)
follows immediately form there, as it is a lower order term. For
T_{12}
note that by Lemma
B.1
, the function
g(a,t)
has continuous derivative with respect to
a
which makes
g
Lipschitz on the ball
\|a\|\leq 1
. Hence we have:

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i}))(g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i})-g(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},\hat{\eta}_{i}))^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau,\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\left\|\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i})\right\|\left\|g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i})-g_{n}(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},\hat{\eta}_{i})\right\|\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau,\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\left\|\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i})\right\|\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})\,.

Now, to establish convergence of
T_{11}
we further expand it as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i}))(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i}))^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau,\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(0,\eta_{i}))(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(0,\eta_{i}))^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau,\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(0,\eta_{i}))(g(0,\eta_{i})-g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i}))^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau,\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}(g(0,\eta_{i})-g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i}))(g(0,\eta_{i})-g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i}))^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau,\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle:=T_{111}+T_{112}+T_{113}

From law of large numbers we first conclude:

T_{111}\overset{P}{\longrightarrow}\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,1*}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,1*}\,\middle|\,\eta)\right)\left(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,1*}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,1*}\,\middle|\,\eta)\right)^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\eta|\leq\tau}\right]

where the factor
1/3
comes due to data splitting. To complete the proof we show
T_{112}=o_{p}(1)
and
T_{113}=o_{p}(1)
follows immediately being a higher order term. We analyse
T_{112}
as follows:

\displaystyle\|T_{112}\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(0,\eta_{i}))(g(0,\eta_{i})-g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i}))^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau,\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(0,\eta_{i}))(g(0,\eta_{i})-g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i}))^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau,\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n},|\hat{\eta}_{i}-\eta_{i}|\leq 1}\right\|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left\|\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(0,\eta_{i}))(g(0,\eta_{i})-g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i}))^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau,\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n},|\hat{\eta}_{i}-\eta_{i}|>1}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i}\left\|\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(0,\eta_{i})\right\|\left|\eta_{i}-\hat{\eta}_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i}\left\|\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(0,\eta_{i})\right\|\left\|g(0,\eta_{i})-g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i})\right\|\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau,\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n},|\hat{\eta}_{i}-\eta_{i}|>1}

\displaystyle\leq\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i}\left\|\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(0,\eta_{i})\right\|\|Z_{i}\|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i}\left\|\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(0,\eta_{i})\right\|\left\|g(0,\eta_{i})-g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i})\right\|\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau,\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n},|\hat{\eta}_{i}-\eta_{i}|>1}

That the first term is
o_{p}(1)
is immediate. For the second term, note that:

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\left\|\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-g(0,\eta_{i})\right\|\left\|g(0,\eta_{i})-g(0,\hat{\eta}_{i})\right\|=O_{p}(1)

and
\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\right\|\leq a_{n},|\hat{\eta}_{i}-\eta_{i}|>1\right)\longrightarrow 0\,.
This finishes the proof of
T_{1}
, i.e. we have established:

T_{1}\overset{P}{\longrightarrow}\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,1*}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,1*}\,\middle|\,\eta)\right)\left(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,1*}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,1*}\,\middle|\,\eta)\right)^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\eta|\leq\tau}\right]

(B.8)

For
T_{3}
in equation (
B.7
) we have for any
1\leq j,k\leq 1+p_{1}+p_{2}
:

\displaystyle\left|\frac{(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{H})^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{H})}{n}\right|_{j,k}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{\langle(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{H})_{*j},{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{H})_{*k}\rangle}{n}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{\left\|(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{H})_{*j}\right\|^{2}}{n}\frac{\left\|(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{H})_{*k}\right\|^{2}}{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{|x|\leq\tau}\left|g_{j}(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},t)-\tilde{N}_{K}(t)^{\top}\omega_{j,\infty}\right|\times\sup_{|x|\leq\tau}\left|g_{k}(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},t)-\tilde{N}_{K}(t)^{\top}\omega_{k,\infty}\right|

\displaystyle=O_{p}(K^{-2})=o_{p}(1)\hskip 14.45377pt[\text{From equation }\ref{eq:g_spline_approx}]\,.

(B.9)

Similarly for
T_{4}
in equation (
B.7
) and for any
1\leq j,k\leq 1+p_{1}+p_{2}
:

\displaystyle\left|\left(\frac{(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{H})^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{G})}{n}\right)_{j,k}\right|\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{\langle(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{H})_{*j},{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{G})_{*k}\rangle}{n}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{\left\|(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{H})_{*j}\right\|^{2}}{n}\frac{\left\|(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{G})_{*k}\right\|^{2}}{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{|x|\leq\tau}\left|g_{j}(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},t)-\tilde{N}_{K}(t)^{\top}\omega_{j,\infty}\right|\sqrt{\frac{\left\|(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{G})_{*k}\right\|^{2}}{n}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{|x|\leq\tau}\left|g_{j}(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},t)-\tilde{N}_{K}(t)^{\top}\omega_{j,\infty}\right|\times O_{p}(1)=o_{p}(1)\,.

(B.10)

where
\left\|(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{G})_{*k}\right\|^{2}/n=O_{p}(1)
follows from law of large numbers and the uniform spline approximation error is
o_{p}(1)
follows from equation (
B.6
). Finally, for
T_{2}
in equation (

), first recall that
