. We can extend this matrix

The matrix

and

as the basis matrix with

. It is easy to see that for any

:

\displaystyle\frac{(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{G})_{*,j}^{\top}{\sf proj}_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{G})_{*,j}}{n}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\mathbf{W}^{*,f}_{1}-\mathbf{G}^{f})_{*,j}^{\top}{\sf proj}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}^{f}_{K}}(\mathbf{W}^{*,f}_{1}-\mathbf{G}^{f})_{*,j}}{n}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{G})_{*,j}^{\top}{\sf proj}_{\mathbf{N}_{K}}(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}-\mathbf{G})_{*,j}}{n}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{(\mathbf{W}^{*,f}_{1}-\mathbf{G}^{f})_{*,j}^{\top}{\sf proj}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}^{f}_{K}}(\mathbf{W}^{*,f}_{1}-\mathbf{G}^{f})_{*,j}}{n}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)

\displaystyle={\sf tr}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{(\mathbf{W}^{*,f}_{1}-\mathbf{G}^{f})_{*,j}^{\top}{\sf proj}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}^{f}_{K}}(\mathbf{W}^{*,f}_{1}-\mathbf{G}^{f})_{*,j}}{n}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left({\sf tr}\left({\sf proj}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}^{f}_{K}}\mathbb{E}\left((\mathbf{W}^{*,f}_{1}-\mathbf{G}^{f})_{*,j}(\mathbf{W}^{*,f}_{1}-\mathbf{G}^{f})_{*,j}^{\top}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\hat{\bm{\eta}},\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)\right)\right)\hskip 7.22743pt[\hat{\bm{\eta}}=\{\hat{\eta}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n/3}\in\mathcal{D}_{3}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K+3}{n}\sup_{|t|\leq\tau}{\sf var}\left(\begin{bmatrix}S&X&b^{\prime}(\eta)Z\end{bmatrix}\,\middle|\,\hat{\eta}=t,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}=O_{p}\left(\frac{K}{n}\right)=o_{p}(1)\,.

(B.11)

where we use Lemma

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\mathbf{W}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{N}_{K,a}}\mathbf{W}}{n}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{P}{\longrightarrow}\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{E}\left[{\sf var}\left(\begin{bmatrix}s&\mathbf{x}&\mathbf{z}b^{\prime}(\eta)\end{bmatrix}\,\middle|\,\eta\right)\mathds{1}_{|\eta|\leq\tau}\right]+\frac{1}{3}\begin{bmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
0&0&\Sigma_{Z}\end{bmatrix}:=\frac{1}{3}\Omega_{\tau}

As

B.2
Proof of Step 2

Define
\mathcal{A}_{n}
to be generated by
\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2}
and
\{(X_{i},Z_{i},\eta_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n/3}
in
\mathcal{D}_{3}
. We start with the following decomposition:

\frac{\mathbf{W}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\top}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K,a}}}{\sqrt{n}}\begin{pmatrix}{\bm{\epsilon}}\\
\eta\end{pmatrix}=\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\mathbf{W}_{2}^{\top}\eta}{\sqrt{n}}

The
asymptotic linear expansion
of the second summand is immediate:

\frac{\mathbf{W}_{2}^{\top}\eta}{\sqrt{n}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\begin{bmatrix}0&0&\eta_{i}Z_{i}\end{bmatrix}

Recall from the definition of
\mathbf{G}
that the
\mathbf{G}_{i*}=g(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},\hat{\eta}_{i})
. Define another matrix
\mathbf{G}^{*}
as
\mathbf{G}^{*}_{i}=g(0,\eta_{i})
. For the first summand, we decompose it as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{N}_{K}}{\epsilon}}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{{}^{*}{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{N}_{K}}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{(\mathbf{W}_{1}-\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*})^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{N}_{K}}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*}-\mathbf{G}^{*})^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{(\mathbf{W}_{1}-\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*})^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{N}_{K}}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle\hskip 80.00012pt+\frac{(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*}-\mathbf{G})^{\top}{\sf proj}_{\tilde{N}_{K}}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{H})^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{N}_{K}}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{(\mathbf{G}^{*}-\mathbf{G})^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle:=T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4}+T_{5}

(B.12)

We show that
T_{2},T_{3},T_{4},T_{5}
are all
o_{p}(1)
. This will establish:

\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{N}_{K}}{\epsilon}}{\sqrt{n}}=\frac{(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*}-\mathbf{G}^{*})^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{\sqrt{n}}+o_{p}(1)\,.

(B.13)

For
T_{2}
note that for any
p_{1}+2\leq j\leq p_{1}+p_{2}+1
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\frac{(\mathbf{W}_{1}-\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*})_{*j}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{N}_{K}}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[(\mathbf{W}_{1}-\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*})_{*j}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{N}_{K}}\mathbb{E}\left({\bm{\epsilon}}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{A}_{n}\right){\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{N}_{K}}(\mathbf{W}_{1}-\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*})_{*j}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\eta}{\sf var}\left({\epsilon}\,\middle|\,\eta\right)\times\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(\mathbf{W}_{1}-\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*})_{*j}^{\top}(\mathbf{W}_{1}-\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*})_{*j}}{n}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})\right]

\displaystyle=\sup_{\eta}{\sf var}\left({\epsilon}\,\middle|\,\eta\right)\times o_{p}(1)\hskip 5.69046pt[\text{From equation }\eqref{eq:W*}]

Now for
T_{3}
, for any
1\leq j\leq 1+p_{1}+p_{2}
by similar calculations:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*}-\mathbf{G})^{\top}{\sf proj}_{\tilde{N}_{K}}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{A}_{n}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\eta}{\sf var}\left({\epsilon}\,\middle|\,\eta\right)\times\frac{(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*}-\mathbf{G})^{\top}{\sf proj}_{\tilde{N}_{K}}(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*}-\mathbf{G})}{n}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\eta}{\sf var}\left({\epsilon}\,\middle|\,\eta\right)\times o_{p}(1)\hskip 14.45377pt[\text{From equation }\eqref{eq:W*-G}]

For
T_{4}
, for
1\leq j\leq 1+p_{1}+p_{2}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{H})^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{N}_{K}}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{A}_{n}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\eta}{\sf var}\left({\epsilon}\,\middle|\,\eta\right)\times\frac{(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{H})^{\top}(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{H})}{n}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\eta}{\sf var}\left({\epsilon}\,\middle|\,\eta\right)\times o_{p}(1)\hskip 14.45377pt[\text{From equation }\eqref{eq:G-H}]

Now for
T_{5}
, using similar technique we have for any
1\leq j\leq p_{1}+p_{2}+1
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{G}^{*})_{*j}^{\top}{\epsilon}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{A}_{n}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\eta}{\sf var}\left({\epsilon}\,\middle|\,\eta\right)\times\frac{\|\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{G}^{*}\|^{2}}{n}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\eta}{\sf var}\left({\epsilon}\,\middle|\,\eta\right)\times\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\left(g_{j}(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},\hat{\eta}_{i})-g_{j}(0,\eta_{i})\right)^{2}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\eta}_{i}\|\leq\tau}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\eta}{\sf var}\left({\epsilon}\,\middle|\,\eta\right)\times\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\left(g_{j}(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},\hat{\eta}_{i})-g_{j}(0,\eta_{i})\right)^{2}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\eta}_{i}\|\leq\tau,\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left.\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\left(g_{j}(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},\hat{\eta}_{i})-g_{j}(0,\eta_{i})\right)^{2}\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\eta}_{i}\|\leq\tau,\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|>a_{n}}\right]

The first term inside the square bracket is
o_{p}(1)
from the boundedness of the partial derivatives of
g
with respect to both
a
and
t
. The second term inside the square bracket inside the square bracket is
o_{p}(1)
because
(1/n)\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\left(g_{j}(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},\hat{\eta}_{i})-g_{j}(0,\eta_{i})\right)^{2}=O_{p}(1)
and
\mathbb{P}(\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|>a_{n})=o(1)
.

Finally we show that:

\displaystyle\frac{(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*}-\mathbf{G}^{*})^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-\mathbf{G}^{*}_{i*}\right){\epsilon}_{i}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-\mathbf{G}^{*}_{i*}\right){\epsilon}_{i}\mathds{1}_{|\eta_{i}|\leq\tau}+o_{p}(1)

(B.14)

This along with equation (
B.13
) concludes:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{N}_{K}}{\epsilon}}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i*}-\mathbf{G}^{*}_{i*}\right){\epsilon}_{i}\mathds{1}_{|\eta_{i}|\leq\tau}+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{\begin{bmatrix}S_{i}&X_{i}&b^{\prime}(\eta_{i})Z_{i}\end{bmatrix}-\mathbb{E}\left[\begin{bmatrix}S_{i}&X_{i}&b^{\prime}(\eta_{i})Z_{i}\end{bmatrix}\,\middle|\,\eta=\eta_{i}\right]\right\}{\epsilon}_{i}\mathds{1}_{|\eta_{i}|\leq\tau}+o_{p}(1)

Taking the function
\varphi
as:

\displaystyle\varphi(X_{i},Z_{i},\eta_{i},\nu_{i})

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{bmatrix}S_{i}&X_{i}&b^{\prime}(\eta_{i})Z_{i}\end{bmatrix}-\mathbb{E}\left[\begin{bmatrix}S_{i}&X_{i}&b^{\prime}(\eta_{i})Z_{i}\end{bmatrix}\,\middle|\,\eta=\eta_{i}\right]\right\}{\epsilon}_{i}\mathds{1}_{|\eta_{i}|\leq\tau}+\begin{bmatrix}0&0&Z_{i}\eta_{i}\end{bmatrix}

we conlcude the proof of Step 2.
All it remains to prove is equation (
B.14
). Define the event
\Delta_{i}
as:

\Delta_{i}=\{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau\cap|\eta_{i}|>\tau\}\cup\{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|>\tau\cap|\eta_{i}|\leq\tau\}\,.

Now for any
1\leq j\leq 1+p_{1}+p_{2}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\left(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i,j}-\mathbf{G}^{*}_{i,j}\right){\epsilon}_{i}\left(\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau}-\mathds{1}_{|\eta_{i}|\leq\tau}\right)\right)^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right]

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\left(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i,j}-\mathbf{G}^{*}_{i,j}\right){\epsilon}_{i}\left(\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau}-\mathds{1}_{|\eta_{i}|\leq\tau}\right)\right)^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{A}_{n}\right]\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\eta}{\sf var}\left({\epsilon}\,\middle|\,\eta\right)\times\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i,j}-\mathbf{G}^{*}_{i,j}\right)^{2}\mathds{1}_{\Delta_{i}}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\eta}{\sf var}\left({\epsilon}\,\middle|\,\eta\right)\times\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,i,j}-\mathbf{G}^{*}_{i,j}\right)^{4}\right]\mathbb{P}\left(\Delta_{i}|\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}\left(\Delta_{i}|\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)}=o_{p}(1)\,.

B.3
Proof of Step 3

From the definition of
\varphi
is Step 2 and the definition of
\Omega_{\tau}^{*}
, Step 3 immediately follows from a direct application of Central Limit theorem.

B.4
Proof of Step 4

In this subsection we prove that:

\frac{\mathbf{W}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K,a}}}{\sqrt{n}}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{R}\\
0\end{pmatrix}=\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{R}}{\sqrt{n}}=o_{p}(1)\,.

From our discussion in Section
2
, the residual vector can be expressed as the sum of three residual term
\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{R}_{1}+\mathbf{R}_{2}+\mathbf{R}_{3}
, where

\mathbf{R}_{1,i}=\frac{1}{2}(\hat{\eta}_{i}-\eta_{i})^{2}b^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\eta}_{i}),\mathbf{R}_{2,i}=(b^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i})-\hat{b}^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i}))(\hat{\eta}_{i}-\eta_{i})
and
\mathbf{R}_{3,i}=(b(\hat{\eta}_{i})-\tilde{N}_{K}(\hat{\eta}_{i})^{\top}\omega_{b,\infty,n})
. We show that each all these terms is asymptotically negligible. For the first term, we can write is as:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n_{3}}\mathbf{R}_{1,i}^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\mathbb{E}\left[(\hat{\eta}_{i}-\eta_{i})^{4}\left(b^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\eta}_{i})\right)^{2}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\mathbb{E}\left[(\hat{\eta}_{i}-\eta_{i})^{4}\left(b^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\eta}_{i})\right)^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{12}\|b^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}^{2}\times n\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|^{4}\times\mathbb{E}[\|Z\|^{4}]=O_{p}(n^{-1})\,.

This implies
\|\mathbf{R}_{1}\|_{2}=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n_{3}}R_{1,i}^{2}}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
and consequently we have for any
1\leq j\leq 1+d_{1}+d_{2}
:

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1,*j}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{R}_{1}}{\sqrt{n}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1,*j}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{W}_{1,*j}}{n}\|\mathbf{R}_{1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(1)\times O_{p}(n^{-1/2})=o_{p}(1)\,.

where
\mathbf{W}_{1,*j}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{W}_{1,*j}/n=O_{p}(1)
has been proved in the proof of Step 1 (see equation (
B.1
)). For the second residual term, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n_{3}}R_{2,i}^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}R_{2,i}^{2}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\mathbb{E}\left[(b^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i})-\hat{b}^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i}))^{2}(\hat{\eta}_{i}-\eta_{i})^{2}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\mathbb{E}\left[(b^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i})-\hat{b}^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i}))^{2}\|Z_{i}\|^{2}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n}{3}\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|^{2}\sup_{|t|\leq\tau}\left|\hat{b}^{\prime}(t)-b^{\prime}(t)\right|\mathbb{E}\left[\|Z\|^{2}\right]

Now from Proposition
2.1
, we have
\sup_{|t|\leq\tau}\left|\hat{b}^{\prime}(t)-b^{\prime}(t)\right|=o_{p}(1)
and from OLS properties we have:
n\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|^{2}=O_{p}(1)
. Combining this, we conclude that
\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}R_{2,i}^{2}\right]=o_{p}(1)
. Now we have:

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1,*j}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{R}_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{W_{1,*j}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}W_{1,*j}}{n}\|\mathbf{R}_{2}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(1)\times o_{p}(1)=o_{p}(1)\,.

For the final residual term
\mathbf{R}_{3}
(residual obtained by approximating the mean function via B-spline basis) define
\mathbf{R}_{3}^{f}
to be the extended version of
\mathbf{R}_{3}
putting 0 in the places where
|\hat{\eta}_{i}|>\tau
, i.e.
\mathbf{R}_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}}
, whereas
\mathbf{R}_{3}^{f}\in\mathbb{R}^{n/3}
. Using this we have:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1,*j}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{R}_{3}}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\mathbf{W}_{1,*j}-\mathbf{G}_{*,j})^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{R}_{3}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{(\mathbf{G}_{*,j}-\mathbf{H}_{*,j})^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{R}_{3}}{\sqrt{n}}

where
\mathbf{G},\mathbf{H}
are same as defined in Subsection
B.1
(just after equation (
B.6
)). For the first summand above, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{(\mathbf{W}_{1,*j}-\mathbf{G}_{*j})^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{N}_{K}}\mathbf{R}_{3}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})\right]

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{(\mathbf{W}^{f}_{1,*j}-\mathbf{G}^{f}_{*j})^{\top}\left(I-{\sf proj}_{\tilde{N}^{f}_{K}}\right)\mathbf{R}^{f}_{3}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{R}_{3}^{f^{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{N}^{f}_{K}}(\mathbf{W}^{f}_{1,*j}-\mathbf{G}_{*j})(\mathbf{W}^{f}_{1,*j}-\mathbf{G}^{f}_{*j})^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{N}^{f}_{K}}\mathbf{R}^{f}_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{R}_{3}^{f^{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{N}^{f}_{K}}\mathbb{E}\left[(\mathbf{W}^{f}_{1,*j}-\mathbf{G}^{f}_{*j})(\mathbf{W}^{f}_{1,j}-\mathbf{G}^{f}_{*j})^{\top}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\hat{\bm{\eta}},\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})\right]{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{N}^{f}_{K}}\mathbf{R}^{f}_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{|t|\leq\tau}{\sf var}\left(\begin{bmatrix}S&X&b^{\prime}(\eta)Z\end{bmatrix}\,\middle|\,\hat{\eta}=t,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbf{R}_{3}\|^{2}/n\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{|t|\leq\tau}{\sf var}\left(\begin{bmatrix}S&X&b^{\prime}(\eta)Z\end{bmatrix}\,\middle|\,\hat{\eta}=t,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)\times\sup_{|t|\leq\tau}\left|\hat{b}^{\prime}(t)-b(t)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{|t|\leq\tau}V(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},t)\times\sup_{|t|\leq\tau}\left|\hat{b}^{\prime}(t)-b(t)\right|^{2}=o_{p}(1)\,.

where
\hat{\bm{\eta}}
is
\{\hat{\eta}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n/3}
from
\mathcal{D}_{3}
. The last line follows from continuity of
V(a,t)
(Lemma
B.1
) and Proposition
2.1
.

\displaystyle\frac{(\mathbf{G}_{*,j}-\mathbf{H}_{*,j})^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{R}_{3}}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\mathbf{G}_{*j}-\mathbf{H}_{*j}\right\|\|\mathbf{R}_{3}\|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}\sqrt{\frac{\left\|\mathbf{G}_{*j}-\mathbf{H}_{*j}\right\|^{2}}{n}\frac{\|\mathbf{R}_{3}\|^{2}}{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{n}\sup_{|x|\leq\tau}\left|g_{j}(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0},x)-\tilde{N}_{K}(x)^{\top}\omega_{j,\infty}\right|\times\sup_{|x|\leq\tau}\left|b(x)-\tilde{N}_{K}(x)^{\top}\delta_{0,n}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{n}/K^{4}=o(1)\,.

The last approximation follows from Theorem
F.1
(with
l=r=0
for
g_{j}
and
l=2,r=0
for
b
) along with Remark
2.2
, which completes the proof of the asymptotic negligibility of the residuals.

Appendix C
Discussion on semi-parametric efficiency and proof of Theorem
3.7

C.1
Some basics of semi-parametric efficiency calculations

We first present a brief sketch of our approach for the convenience of the general reader. Our proof is based on the techniques introduced in Section 3.3. of
[
8
]
, albeit we sketch the main ideas here for the convenience of the readers. Suppose
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}\sim P_{0}\in\mathcal{P}
with density function
p_{0}
. We will work with
\sqrt{p_{0}}
instead of
p_{0}
itself, as
s_{0}:=\sqrt{p_{0}}
lies on the unit sphere of
L_{2}(\mathbb{R})
(with respect to Lebesgue measure) and gives rise to a nice Hilbert space. Suppose, we are interested in estimating a one dimensional functional
\theta(s_{0})
, where
\theta:L_{2}(\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{R}
. As initially pointed out by Stein
[
42
]
, estimating any one dimensional functional of some non-parametric component is at least as hard as estimating the functional by restricting oneself to an one-dimensional parametric sub-model that contains the true parameter. In general, any one dimensional smooth parametrization, i.e. a function from say
\varphi:(-t_{0},t_{0})\to L_{2}(\mathbb{R})
(
t_{0}>0
) with
\varphi(0)=s_{0}
introduces a one dimensional parametric sub-model, which essentially is a curve on the unit sphere of
L_{2}(\mathbb{R})
passing through
s_{0}
. We restrict ourselves to
regular parametrizations
, which are differentiable on
(-t_{0},t_{0})
in the following sense: for any
|t|<t_{0}
, there exists some function
\dot{s}_{\varphi,t}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
such that,

\lim_{h\to 0}\left\|\frac{\varphi(t+h)-\varphi(t)}{h}-\dot{s}_{\varphi,t}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=0\,.

with
\|\dot{s}_{\varphi,t}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}>0
. Let
\mathcal{G}
be the set of all such regular parametrizations. Under mild conditions, this derivative also coincides with the pointwise derivative of
\varphi(t)
with respect to
t
, i.e.
\dot{s}_{\varphi,t}(x)=(d/dt)\varphi(t)(x)
. As those conditions are easily satisfied in our model, henceforth we use this fact in our derivations. Define the
tangent set
of
\mathcal{P}
at
P_{0}
as
\dot{\mathcal{P}}_{P_{0}}=\{\dot{s}_{\varphi,0}:\varphi\in\mathcal{G}\}
and the tangent space
T(P_{0})=\overline{lin}(\dot{\mathcal{P}}_{P_{0}})
, the closed linear subspace spanned by
\dot{\mathcal{P}}_{P_{0}}
.

We restrict the discussion to the functional
\theta
that obeys the
pathwise norm differentiabilty
condition, which asserts the existence of a bounded linear functional
\L:T(P_{0})\to\mathbb{R}
such that, for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{G}
:

\L(\dot{s}_{\varphi,0}):=\lim_{t\to 0}\frac{\theta(\varphi(t))-\theta(\varphi(0))}{t}\,.

Now, for any fixed
\varphi\in\mathcal{G}
, the collection
\{P_{t}\}_{|t|<t_{0}}
is the one-dimensional regular parametric sub-model, where
P_{t}
is the probability measure corresponding to
\varphi(t)
. Hence for this fixed
\varphi
, one may also view
\tilde{\theta}_{\varphi}
as a function from
(-t_{0},t_{0})\mapsto\mathbb{R}
via the identification
\tilde{\theta}_{\varphi}(t)=\theta(\varphi(t))
and our parameter of interest is
\tilde{\theta}_{\varphi}(0)
. The information bound (henceforth denoted by IB) for estimating
\tilde{\theta}_{\varphi}(0)
is:

\displaystyle IB(\varphi)=\frac{\left(\tilde{\theta}_{\varphi}^{\prime}(0)\right)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{d}{dt}\log{p_{t}(X)}\mid_{t=0}\right)^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(\frac{d}{dt}\tilde{\theta}_{\varphi}(t)\mid_{t=0}\right)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{d}{dt}\log{s^{2}_{t}(X)}\mid_{t=0}\right)^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(\frac{d}{dt}\theta(\varphi(t))\mid_{t=0}\right)^{2}}{4\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{d}{dt}\log{s_{t}(X)}\mid_{t=0}\right)^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(\L(\dot{s}_{\varphi,0})\right)^{2}}{4\int\dot{s}_{\varphi,0}(x)^{2}\ dx}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(\L(\dot{s}_{\varphi,0})\right)^{2}}{\|\dot{s}_{\varphi,0}\|^{2}_{F}}=\L^{2}\left(\frac{\dot{s}_{\varphi,0}}{\|\dot{s}_{\varphi,0}\|_{F}}\right)

where
\|\cdot\|_{F}=2\|\cdot\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R})}
is the Fisher norm (
[
40
]
,
[
47
]
) and the last equality follows from the fact that
\L
is a bounded linear operator.
The
optimal asymptotic variance
(a term borrowed from
[
45
]
) for estimating
\theta(s_{0})
is defined as the supremum of all these Cramer-Rao lower bounds
IB(\varphi)
over all regular one dimensional parametrization
\varphi\in\mathcal{G}
, i.e.:

Optimal asymptotic variance

\displaystyle=\sup_{\dot{s}_{\varphi,0}\in T(P_{0})}\L^{2}\left(\frac{\dot{s}_{\varphi,0}}{\|\dot{s}_{\varphi,0}\|_{F}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\sup_{\dot{s}_{\varphi,0}\in T(P_{0}):\|\dot{s}_{\varphi,0}\|_{F}=1}\L\left(\dot{s}_{\varphi,0}\right)\right)^{2}=\|\L\|_{*}^{2}\,.

where
\|\cdot\|_{*}
is the dual norm of the functional
\L
on
T(P_{0})
with respect to Fisher norm. As
\L
is a bounded linear functional on the Hilbert space
T(p_{0})
, by Reisz representation theorem, there exists some
s^{*}\in T(p_{0})
such that:

\L(\dot{s}_{\varphi,0})=\langle s^{*},\dot{s}_{\varphi,0}\rangle_{F}\ \ \forall\ \ \dot{s}_{\varphi,0}\in T(P_{0})\,.

where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{F}
is
2\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R})}
. This further implies
\|\L\|_{*}=\|s^{*}\|_{F}
and consequently, the information bound corresponding to the hardest one dimensional parametric sub-model is:

\text{Optimal asymptotic variance}=\|s^{*}\|_{F}^{2}\,.

Therefore the problem of estimating the efficient information bound boils down to finding the representer
s^{*}
in the Tangent space
T(P_{0})
. To summarize, the key steps are:

1.

First quantify
T(P_{0})
in the given model.

2.

Then find the expression for
\L
by differentiating
\theta(\varphi(t))
with respect to
t
.

3.

Finally use the identity
\L(\dot{s}_{\varphi,0})=\langle s^{*},\dot{s}_{\varphi,0}\rangle_{F}
for all
\dot{s}_{\varphi,0}\in T(P_{0})
to find
s^{*}
.

A detailed proof of the efficiency of our estimator under normality is presented in Section
C.2
, but here we sketch the main idea to give readers a sense of the application of the above approach into our model. The log - likelihood function of our model, for any generic observation
(Y,Q,X,Z)
, can be written as:

\displaystyle\ell(\vartheta)

\displaystyle=2\log{s_{0}\left(Y\mid Q,X,Z\right)}+\log{s_{0}\left(Q\mid X,Z\right)}+\log{s_{0}(X,Z)}

\displaystyle=4\left\{\log{\phi^{1/2}\left(Y-\alpha-X^{\top}\beta-b(Q-Z^{\top}\gamma)\right)}+\log{s_{\eta}(Q-Z^{\top}\gamma)}+\log{s_{X,Z}(X,Z)}\right\}

where
\phi
is the Gaussian density,
s_{\eta}
and
s_{X,Z}
are square-roots of the densities of
\eta
and
(X,Z)
respectively and
\vartheta
is the collection of all unknown parameters, i.e.
\vartheta=(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,b,s_{\eta},s_{X,Z})
.
We are interested in the functional
\theta(\varphi(t))=\alpha_{\varphi(t)}
which implies that the derivative
\L(\dot{\alpha}_{0})=\dot{\alpha}_{\varphi}=(d/dt)\alpha_{\varphi(t)}\mid_{t=0}
. Hence, the representer
s^{*}\in T(P_{0})
should satisfy:

\langle s^{*},\dot{s}_{\varphi,0}\rangle_{F}=\dot{\alpha}_{\gamma}\,,

(C.1)

for all

\varphi\in\mathcal{G}
. As a consequence, the optimal asymptotic variance will be
\alpha^{*}=\langle s^{*},s^{*}\rangle_{F}
. In the proof (Section
C.2
of the Supplementary document), we use the identity (
C.1
) for some suitably chosen
\varphi\in\mathcal{G}
(or equivalently
\dot{s}_{\varphi,0}\in T(P_{0})
) to obtain
\alpha^{*}
.

C.2
Proof of Theorem
3.7

The model we consider here is:

\displaystyle Y_{i}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{0}S_{i}+X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}+b_{0}(\eta_{i})+\epsilon_{i}

\displaystyle Q_{i}

\displaystyle=Z_{i}^{\top}\gamma_{0}+\eta_{i}\,.

where
S_{i}=\mathds{1}_{Q_{i}\geq 0}
and
\epsilon\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\tau^{2})
. For simplicity we assume here
\tau^{2}=1
. An inspection to our proof immediately reveals that extension to general
\tau^{2}
is straight forward. Statisticians observe
\{D_{i}\triangleq(Y_{i},X_{i},Z_{i},Q_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
at stage
n
of the experiment and hence the likelihood of the parameters
\theta\triangleq(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,b(\cdot),s_{\eta},s_{X,Z})
becomes:

\displaystyle L\left(\theta\,\middle|\,D\right)

\displaystyle=\Pi_{i=1}^{n}\left[p\left(Y_{i}\mid Q_{i},X_{i},Z_{i}\right)\times p(Q_{i}\mid X_{i},Z_{i})\times p(X_{i},Z_{i})\right]

As we calculate the information bound, henceforth we only will deal with one observation and generically write:

\displaystyle\ell(\theta)=\log{L(\theta)}

\displaystyle=\log{p_{0}(Y,Q,X,Z)}

\displaystyle=\log{p_{0}\left(Y\mid Q,X,Z\right)}+\log{p_{0}\left(Q\mid X,Z\right)}+\log{p_{0}(X,Z)}

\displaystyle=2\log{s_{0}\left(Y\mid Q,X,Z\right)}+\log{s_{0}\left(Q\mid X,Z\right)}+\log{s_{0}(X,Z)}

Now consider some parametrization
\gamma\in\mathcal{R}
where
\mathcal{R}
is the set of all regular parametric model as mentioned in Subsection
C.1
the derivative of log-likelihood along this curve at
t=0
can be written as:

\displaystyle S_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\log{p_{\gamma(t)}(Y,Q,X,Z)}|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=2\left[\frac{\dot{s}_{\gamma,0}\left(Y\mid Q,X,Z\right)}{s_{0}\left(Y\mid Q,X,Z\right)}+\frac{\dot{s}_{\gamma,0}\left(Q\mid X,Z\right)}{s_{0}\left(Q\mid X,Z\right)}+\frac{\dot{s}_{\gamma,0}(X,Z)}{s_{0}(X,Z)}\right]

and as a consequence, Fisher information for estimating
\alpha_{0}
along this parametric submodel curve:

\displaystyle I(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\left\|\dot{s}_{\gamma,0}\right\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(S^{2}(\gamma)\right)

\displaystyle=4\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\dot{s}_{\gamma,0}\left(Y\mid Q,X,Z\right)}{s_{0}\left(Y\mid Q,X,Z\right)}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{\dot{s}_{\gamma,0}\left(Q\mid X,Z\right)}{s_{0}\left(Q\mid X,Z\right)}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{\dot{s}_{\gamma,0}(X,Z)}{s_{0}(X,Z)}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=4\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\phi_{1}^{\prime}(\epsilon)\left[-\dot{\alpha}S-X^{\top}\dot{\beta}-\dot{b}(\eta)+b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\dot{\gamma}\right]}{\phi_{1}(\epsilon)}\right]^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\dot{s_{\eta}}(\eta)-s_{\eta}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\dot{\gamma}}{s_{\eta}(\eta)}\right]^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\dot{s}_{X,Z}(X,Z)}{s_{X,Z}(X,Z)}\right]^{2}\right\}

where
\phi_{1}
is the square root of the density of standard gaussian distribution,
s_{\eta}
is the square root of the density of
\eta
and
s_{X,Z}
is the joint density of
(X,Z)
. The function
\dot{s}_{\eta}
(resp.
\dot{s}_{X,Z}
) is defined as the
(d/dt)s_{\eta,\gamma(t)}\mid_{t=0}
(resp.
(d/dt)s_{X,Z,\gamma(t)}\mid_{t=0}
). Similar definition holds for
\dot{\alpha},\dot{\beta},\dot{\gamma}
, where we omit the subscript
\gamma
for notational simplicity. The function
s_{\eta}^{\prime}
here denotes the derivative of
s_{\eta,0}(x)
(true data generating density) with respect to
x
. Note that in the last equality we reparametrize the variable
(Y,Q,X,Z)\to({\epsilon},\eta,X,Z)
which is bijective. The fisher inner product in
T(P_{0})
corresponding to two parametrization
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
can be expressed as:

\displaystyle\langle\dot{s}_{\gamma_{1},0},\dot{s}_{\gamma_{2},0}\rangle_{F}

\displaystyle=4\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\frac{\dot{s}_{\gamma_{1},0}\left(Y\mid Q,X,Z\right)}{s_{0}\left(Y\mid Q,X,Z\right)}+\frac{\dot{s}_{\gamma_{1},0}\left(Q\mid X,Z\right)}{s_{0}\left(Q\mid X,Z\right)}+\frac{\dot{s}_{\gamma_{1},0}(X,Z)}{s_{0}(X,Z)}\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\times\left\{\frac{\dot{s}_{\gamma_{2},0}\left(Y\mid Q,X,Z\right)}{s_{0}\left(Y\mid Q,X,Z\right)}+\frac{\dot{s}_{\gamma_{2},0}\left(Q\mid X,Z\right)}{s_{0}\left(Q\mid X,Z\right)}+\frac{\dot{s}_{\gamma_{2},0}(X,Z)}{s_{0}(X,Z)}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=4\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\dot{s}_{\gamma_{1},0}\left(Y\mid Q,X,Z\right)}{s_{0}\left(Y\mid Q,X,Z\right)}\frac{\dot{s}_{\gamma_{2},0}\left(Y\mid Q,X,Z\right)}{s_{0}\left(Y\mid Q,X,Z\right)}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt\left.+\frac{\dot{s}_{\gamma_{1},0}\left(Q\mid X,Z\right)}{s_{0}\left(Q\mid X,Z\right)}\frac{\dot{s}_{\gamma_{2},0}\left(Q\mid X,Z\right)}{s_{0}\left(Q\mid X,Z\right)}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 200.0003pt\left.+\frac{\dot{s}_{\gamma_{1},0}(X,Z)}{s_{0}(X,Z)}\frac{\dot{s}_{\gamma_{2},0}(X,Z)}{s_{0}(X,Z)}\right]

\displaystyle=4\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\phi_{1}^{\prime}({\epsilon})}{\phi_{1}({\epsilon})}\right)^{2}\left\{-\dot{\alpha}^{1}S-X^{\top}\dot{\beta}^{1}-\dot{b}^{1}(\eta)+b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\dot{\gamma}^{1}\right\}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\left.\times\left\{-\dot{\alpha}^{2}S-X^{\top}\dot{\beta}^{2}-\dot{b}^{2}(\eta)+b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\dot{\gamma}^{2}\right\}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\frac{\dot{s_{\eta}}^{1}(\eta)-s_{\eta}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\dot{\gamma}^{1}}{s_{\eta}(\eta)}\right\}\left\{\frac{\dot{s_{\eta}}^{2}(\eta)-s_{\eta}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\dot{\gamma}^{2}}{s_{\eta}(\eta)}\right\}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\frac{\dot{s}_{X,Z}^{1}(X,Z)}{s_{X,Z}(X,Z)}\right\}\left\{\frac{\dot{s}_{X,Z}^{2}(X,Z)}{s_{X,Z}(X,Z)}\right\}\right]\right\}

where the superscript
i\in(1,2)
refers to the parametrization corresponding to
\gamma_{i}
. Our parameter of interest is
\theta(\gamma(t))=\alpha_{\gamma(t)}
. Differentiating with respect to
t
we obtain
L(\dot{s}_{\gamma,0})=\dot{\alpha}_{\gamma,0}:=\dot{\alpha}
. Hence we need to find the representer
s^{*}
such that:

\langle s^{*},\dot{s}_{\gamma,0}\rangle_{F}=\dot{\alpha}\,.

(C.2)

for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{R}
. This further implies:

\displaystyle\dot{\alpha}

\displaystyle=4\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\phi_{1}^{\prime}(\epsilon)}{\phi_{1}(\epsilon)}\right)^{2}\left\{-\dot{\alpha}S-X^{\top}\dot{\beta}-\dot{b}(\eta)+b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\dot{\gamma}\right\}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\left.\times\left\{-\alpha^{*}S-Z^{\top}\beta^{*}-b^{*}(\eta)+b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\gamma^{*}\right\}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\frac{\dot{s_{\eta}}(\eta)-s_{\eta}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\dot{\gamma}}{s_{\eta}(\eta)}\right\}\left\{\frac{s_{\eta}^{*}(\eta)-s_{\eta}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\gamma^{*}}{s_{\eta}(\eta)}\right\}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\frac{\dot{s_{X,Z}}(X,Z)}{s_{X}(X,Z)}\right\}\left\{\frac{s_{X,Z}^{*}(X,Z)}{s_{X}(X,Z)}\right\}\right]\right\}

(C.3)

for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{R}
and the
optimal asymptotic variance
in estimating
\alpha_{0}
is:

\text{Optimal asymptotic variance }=\|s^{*}\|_{F}^{2}=\alpha^{*}\,.

In the rest of the analysis we use equation (
C.2
) repeatedly for different choices of
\dot{s}_{\gamma,0}
to obtain the value of
\alpha^{*}
. First, putting
\dot{\alpha}=\dot{\beta}=\dot{\gamma}=\dot{b}=\dot{s}_{\eta}=0
(as zero vector is always in
T(P_{0})
) we obtain:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\frac{\dot{s}_{X,Z}(X,Z)}{s_{X,Z}(X,Z)}\right\}\left\{\frac{s_{X,Z}^{*}(X,Z)}{s_{X,Z}(X,Z)}\right\}\right]=0\ \ \forall\ \ \dot{s}_{X,Z}\,.

Hence we have
s^{*}_{X,Z}\equiv 0
. Thus we can modify equation (
C.3
) to obtain:

\displaystyle\dot{\alpha}

\displaystyle=4\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\phi_{1}^{\prime}({\epsilon})}{\phi_{1}({\epsilon})}\right)^{2}\left\{-\dot{\alpha}S-X^{\top}\dot{\beta}-\dot{b}(\eta)+b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\dot{\gamma}\right\}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.\left.\times\left\{-\alpha^{*}S-X^{\top}\beta^{*}-b^{*}(\eta_{i})+b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\gamma^{*}\right\}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\frac{\dot{s_{\eta}}(\eta)-s_{\eta}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\dot{\gamma}}{s_{\eta}(\eta)}\right\}\left\{\frac{s_{\eta}^{*}(\eta)-s_{\eta}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\gamma^{*}}{s_{\eta}(\eta)}\right\}\right]\right\}

(C.4)

Next we put
\dot{\alpha}=\dot{\beta}=\dot{\gamma}=\dot{b}=0
in equation (
C.4
) to obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\frac{\dot{s_{\eta}}(\eta)}{s_{\eta}(\eta)}\right\}\left\{\frac{s_{\eta}^{*}(\eta)-s_{\eta}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\gamma^{*}}{s_{\eta}(\eta)}\right\}\right]=0

As
E(X)=0
, we obtain from the above equation:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\frac{\dot{s_{\eta}}(\eta)}{s_{\eta}(\eta)}\right\}\left\{\frac{s_{\eta}^{*}(\eta)-s_{\eta}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\gamma^{*}}{s_{\eta}(\eta)}\right\}\right]=0

As
E(Z^{\top}\gamma^{*})=0
, we can conclude that
s^{*}_{\eta}(\cdot)\equiv 0
. So modifying equation (
C.4
) we get the following equation:

\displaystyle\dot{\alpha}

\displaystyle=4\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\phi_{1}^{\prime}({\epsilon})}{\phi_{1}({\epsilon})}\right)^{2}\left\{-\dot{\alpha}S-X^{\top}\dot{\beta}-\dot{b}(\eta)+b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\dot{\gamma}\right\}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.\left.\times\left\{-\alpha^{*}S-X^{\top}\beta^{*}-b^{*}(\eta)+b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\gamma^{*}\right\}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\dot{\gamma}^{\top}\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{s_{\eta}^{\prime}(\eta)}{s_{\eta}(\eta)}\right)^{2}\Sigma_{Z}\gamma^{*}\right\}

Observe that:

4\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\frac{\phi_{1}^{\prime}({\epsilon})}{\phi_{1}({\epsilon})}\right)^{2}\right\}=\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\frac{d}{d\epsilon}\log{\phi(\epsilon)}\right)^{2}\right\}=1\,.

Hence defining
4\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{s_{\eta}^{\prime}(\eta)}{s_{\eta}(\eta)}\right)^{2}=I_{\eta}
the above equation becomes:

\displaystyle\dot{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\left\{\mathbb{E}_{X,\eta}\left[\left\{-\dot{\alpha}S-X^{\top}\dot{\beta}-\dot{b}(\eta)+b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\dot{\gamma}\right\}\right.\right.

(C.5)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\left.\times\left\{-\alpha^{*}S-X^{\top}\beta^{*}-b^{*}(\eta)+b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\gamma^{*}\right\}\right]+I_{\eta}\dot{\gamma}^{\top}\Sigma_{Z}\gamma^{*}\right\}

Next we put
\dot{\alpha}=\dot{\beta}=\dot{\gamma}=0
in equation (
C.5
) to get:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\dot{b}(\eta)\left\{\alpha^{*}S+X^{\top}\beta^{*}+b^{*}(\eta)-b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\gamma^{*}\right\}\right]=0\,.

Again from the independence of
(X,Z)
and
\eta
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\dot{b}(\eta)\left\{\alpha^{*}S+b^{*}(\eta)\right\}\right]=0\,.

Hence, it is immediately clear the choice of
b^{*}(\eta)=-\alpha^{*}\mathbb{E}\left(S\,\middle|\,\eta\right)
. Using this we modify the equation (
C.5
) as below:

\displaystyle\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\dot{\alpha}S+X^{\top}\dot{\beta}-b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\dot{\gamma}\right\}\left\{\alpha^{*}(S-E(S\,\middle|\,\eta))+X^{\top}\beta^{*}-b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\gamma^{*}\right\}\right]\right.

(C.6)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.+I_{\eta}\dot{\gamma}^{\top}\Sigma_{Z}\gamma^{*}\right\}=\dot{\alpha}

Next putting
\dot{\alpha}=\dot{\beta}=0
in equation (
C.6
) we get:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{-b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\dot{\gamma}\right\}\left\{\alpha^{*}(S-E(S\,\middle|\,\eta))+X^{\top}\beta^{*}-b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\gamma^{*}\right\}\right]+I_{\eta}\dot{\gamma}^{\top}\Sigma_{Z}\gamma^{*}=0

which further implies:

\displaystyle\dot{\gamma}^{\top}\left[\alpha^{*}\mathbb{E}_{\eta}\left\{-b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)\mathbb{E}(ZS\,\middle|\,\eta)\right\}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left\{-b^{\prime}_{0}(\eta)\right\}\dot{\gamma}^{\top}\Sigma_{ZX}\beta^{*}

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt+\left[\mathbb{E}\left\{(b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta))^{2}\right\}+I_{\eta}\right]\dot{\gamma}^{\top}\Sigma_{Z}\gamma^{*}=0

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\alpha^{*}\dot{\gamma}^{\top}v_{1}+c_{1}\dot{\gamma}^{\top}\Sigma_{ZX}\beta^{*}+c_{2}\dot{\gamma}^{\top}\Sigma_{Z}\gamma^{*}=0

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\alpha^{*}v_{1}+c_{1}\Sigma_{ZX}\beta^{*}+c_{2}\Sigma_{Z}\gamma^{*}=0\,.

(C.8)

Here
v_{1}=\mathbb{E}_{\eta}\left\{-b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)\mathbb{E}(ZS\,\middle|\,\eta)\right\}
,
c_{1}=\mathbb{E}\left\{-b^{\prime}_{0}(\eta)\right\}
and
c_{2}=\left[\mathbb{E}\left\{(b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta))^{2}\right\}+I_{\eta}\right]
. Now equation (
C.6
) will be modified to:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\dot{\alpha}S+X^{\top}\dot{\beta}\right\}\left\{\alpha^{*}(S-\mathbb{E}(S\,\middle|\,\eta))+X^{\top}\beta^{*}-b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\gamma^{*}\right\}\right]=\dot{\alpha}

(C.9)

Putting
\dot{\alpha}=0
in equation (
) we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{X^{\top}\dot{\beta}\right\}\left\{\alpha^{*}(S-E(S\mid\eta))+X^{\top}\beta^{*}-b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\gamma^{*}\right\}\right]=0

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\dot{\beta}^{\top}\left[\alpha^{*}\mathbb{E}\left(XS\right)\right]+\dot{\beta}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}\beta^{*}+\mathbb{E}(-b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta))\dot{\beta}^{\top}\Sigma_{XZ}\gamma^{*}=0

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\alpha^{*}\dot{\beta}^{\top}v_{2}+\dot{\beta}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}\beta^{*}+c_{1}\dot{\beta}^{\top}\Sigma_{XZ}\gamma^{*}=0

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\alpha^{*}v_{2}+\Sigma_{X}\beta^{*}+c_{1}\Sigma_{XZ}\gamma^{*}=0\,.

(C.10)

where
v_{2}=E(XS)
. Hence equation (
) will be modified to:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\dot{\alpha}S\right\}\left\{\alpha^{*}(S-E(S\mid\eta))+X^{\top}\beta^{*}-b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\gamma^{*}\right\}\right]=\dot{\alpha}

(C.11)

which implies:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[s_{i}\left\{\alpha^{*}(S-E(S\mid\eta))+X^{\top}\beta^{*}-b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)Z^{\top}\gamma^{*}\right\}\right]=1

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\alpha^{*}\mathbb{E}_{\eta}\left\{{\sf var}(S\mid\eta)\right\}+\mathbb{E}(SX^{\top})\beta^{*}+\mathbb{E}_{\eta}\left\{-b_{0}^{\prime}(\eta)\mathbb{E}(SZ^{\top}\mid\eta_{i})\right\}\gamma^{*}=1

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\alpha^{*}c_{3}+v_{2}^{\top}\beta^{*}+v_{1}^{\top}\gamma^{*}=1\,.

(C.12)

where
c_{3}=\mathbb{E}_{\eta}\left\{{\sf var}(S\mid\eta)\right\}
. Finally we have three unknowns (
\alpha^{*},\beta^{*},\gamma^{*}
) and three equations (equation (
C.8
), (
C.10
) and (
C.12
)), which we solve to get the value of
\alpha^{*}
. For the convenience of the readers we write those equations here:

\displaystyle\alpha^{*}v_{1}+c_{1}\Sigma_{ZX}\beta^{*}+c_{2}\Sigma_{Z}\gamma^{*}

\displaystyle=0\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p_{2}}\,,

(C.13)

\displaystyle\alpha^{*}v_{2}+\Sigma_{X}\beta^{*}+c_{1}\Sigma_{XZ}\gamma^{*}

\displaystyle=0\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p_{1}}\,,

(C.14)

\displaystyle\alpha^{*}c_{3}+v_{2}^{\top}\beta^{*}+v_{1}^{\top}\gamma^{*}

\displaystyle=1\in{\mathbb{R}}\,.

(C.15)

where
Z\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p_{2}}
and
X\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p_{1}}
. These three equations can be written in a matrix form as following:

\begin{bmatrix}c_{3}&v_{2}^{\top}&v_{1}^{\top}\\
v_{2}&\Sigma_{X}&c_{1}\Sigma_{XZ}\\
v_{1}&c_{1}\Sigma_{ZX}&c_{2}\Sigma_{Z}\end{bmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\alpha^{*}\\
\beta^{*}\\
\gamma^{*}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}

Hence we have:

\displaystyle\alpha^{*}

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{\top}\begin{bmatrix}c_{3}&v_{2}^{\top}&v_{1}^{\top}\\
v_{2}&\Sigma_{X}&c_{1}\Sigma_{XZ}\\
v_{1}&c_{1}\Sigma_{ZX}&c_{2}\Sigma_{Z}\end{bmatrix}^{-1}e_{1}

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{\top}\begin{bmatrix}c_{3}&v_{2}^{\top}&-v_{1}^{\top}\\
v_{2}&\Sigma_{X}&-c_{1}\Sigma_{XZ}\\
-v_{1}&-c_{1}\Sigma_{ZX}&c_{2}\Sigma_{Z}\end{bmatrix}^{-1}e_{1}

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{\top}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{E}\left({\sf var}\left(S\,\middle|\,\eta\right)\right)&\mathbb{E}(SX^{\top})&\mathbb{E}(SZ^{\top}b^{\prime}(\eta))\\
\mathbb{E}(SX)&\Sigma_{X}&\mathbb{E}(XZ^{\top}b^{\prime}(\eta))\\
\mathbb{E}(SZb^{\prime}(\eta))&\mathbb{E}(ZX^{\top}b^{\prime}(\eta))&\Sigma_{Z}(1+(b^{\prime}(\eta))^{2})\end{pmatrix}^{-1}e_{1}

(C.16)

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{\top}\Omega e_{1}\,.

(C.17)

Remark C.1
.

Note that if
b
is a linear function, (which happens if
(\epsilon,\eta)
is generated from bivariate normal with correlation
\rho
), then
b^{\prime}
is a constant function. Hence the second term in the expression of efficient information vanishes and we get:

I_{eff}=\left[\left\{\mathbb{E}_{\eta}({\sf var}(S\,\middle|\,\eta))-\mathbb{E}(SX^{\top})\Sigma_{X}^{-1}\mathbb{E}(SX)\right\}\right]

which is same as the efficient information of partial linear model. Hence, one may think the second term as the price we pay for non-linearity of
b
.

Appendix D
Proof of Proposition
2.1

Recall that our model can be written as:

\displaystyle Y_{i}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{0}S_{i}+X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}+b(\eta_{i})+\epsilon_{i}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{0}S_{i}+X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}+b(\hat{\eta}_{i})+R_{1,i}+\epsilon_{i}

where
R_{1,i}=b(\eta_{i})-b(\hat{\eta}_{i})
is the residual in approximating
\eta_{i}
by
\hat{\eta}_{i}
. For notational simplicity, we absorb
S_{i}
into
X_{i}
(and
\alpha_{0}
into
\beta_{0}
) and write:

Y_{i}=X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}+b(\hat{\eta}_{i})+R_{1,i}+\epsilon_{i}

(D.1)

As mentioned in Section
2
, we only need to estimate the derivative of the mean function
b
on the interval
[-\tau,\tau]
and consequently we consider only those observations for which
|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau
. We use scaled B-spline basis
\tilde{N}_{K}
to estimate
b
non-parametrically on the interval
[-\tau,\tau]
. For more details on the B-spline basis and its scaled version, see
F
. Define a vector
\omega_{b,\infty,n}
as:

\omega_{b,\infty,n}=\mathop{\rm argmin}_{\omega\in\mathbb{R}^{(K+2)}}\sup_{|x|\leq\tau}\left|b(x)-\tilde{N}_{K}(x)^{\top}\omega\right|

By applying Theorem
F.1
of Section
F
we conclude:

\displaystyle\left\|b(x)-\tilde{N}_{K}(x)^{\top}\omega_{b,\infty,n}\right\|_{\infty,[-\tau,\tau]}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{2\tau}{K}\right)^{3}\left\|b^{\prime\prime\prime}\right\|_{\infty,[-\tau,\tau]}\,,

(D.2)

\displaystyle\left\|b^{\prime}(x)-\nabla\tilde{N}_{K}(x)^{\top}\omega_{b,\infty,n}\right\|_{\infty,[-\tau,\tau]}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{2\tau}{K}\right)^{2}\left\|b^{\prime\prime\prime}\right\|_{\infty,[-\tau,\tau]}\,.

(D.3)

were
\nabla\tilde{N}_{k}(x)
is the vector of derivatives of the co-ordinate of the basis functions in
\tilde{N}_{k}(x)
. Using this spline approximation we further expand on equation (
D.1
):

Y_{i}=X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}+\tilde{N}_{K}(\hat{\eta}_{i})^{\top}\omega_{b,\infty,n}+R_{1i}+R_{2i}+\epsilon_{i}

(D.4)

where
R_{2,i}=b(\hat{\eta}_{i})-\tilde{N}_{K}(\hat{\eta}_{i})^{\top}\omega_{b,\infty,n}
is the spline approximation error. To estimate
w_{b,\infty,n}
we first estimate
\beta_{0}
as:

\hat{\beta}=\left(X^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}X\right)^{-1}X^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}Y\,.

(D.5)

and then estimate
w_{b,\infty,n}
as:

\hat{w}_{b,\infty,n}=\left(\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}\right)^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}(Y-X\hat{\beta})\,.

(D.6)

and consequently we set
\hat{b}(x)=\tilde{N}_{k}(x)^{\top}\hat{\omega}_{b,\infty,n}
and
\hat{b}^{\prime}(x)=\nabla\tilde{N}_{k}(x)^{\top}\hat{\omega}_{b,\infty,n}
. The estimation error of
b^{\prime}
using
\hat{b}^{\prime}
is then bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\left|\hat{b}^{\prime}(x)-b^{\prime}(x)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\nabla\tilde{N}_{k}(x)^{\top}\hat{\omega}_{b,\infty,n}-b^{\prime}(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\nabla\tilde{N}_{k}(x)^{\top}\hat{\omega}_{b,\infty,n}-\nabla\tilde{N}_{k}(x)^{\top}\omega_{b,\infty,n}\right|+\left|\nabla\tilde{N}_{k}(x)^{\top}\omega_{b,\infty,n}-b^{\prime}(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\nabla\tilde{N}_{k}(x)\right\|\left\|\hat{\omega}_{b,\infty,n}-\omega_{b,\infty,n}\right\|+\sup_{|t|\leq\tau}\left|\nabla\tilde{N}_{k}(t)^{\top}\omega_{b,\infty,n}-b^{\prime}(t)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim K\sqrt{K}\left\|\hat{\omega}_{b,\infty,n}-\omega_{b,\infty,n}\right\|+C\left(\frac{2\tau}{K}\right)^{2}\left\|b^{\prime\prime\prime}\right\|_{\infty,[-\tau,\tau]}

(D.7)

where the last inequality follows from the fact that
\|\nabla\tilde{N}_{k}(x)\|\lesssim K\sqrt{K}
(see Lemma
F.5
) and equation (
D.3
). We now relate
\hat{\omega}_{b,\infty,n}
to
\omega_{b,\infty,n}
using equation (
D.4
):

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{b,\infty,n}

\displaystyle=\left(\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}\right)^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}(Y-X\hat{\beta})

\displaystyle=w_{b,\infty,n}+\left(\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}\right)^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}X\left(\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0}\right)+\left(\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}\right)^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{1}

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt+\left(\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}\right)^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{2}+\left(\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}\right)^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}

\displaystyle=w_{b,\infty,n}+\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}X\left(\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0}\right)}{n}+\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{1}}{n}

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt+\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{2}}{n}+\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{n}

\displaystyle=w_{b,\infty,n}+\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}X\left(\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0}\right)}{n}+\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}{n}\right)^{-1}\left[\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{1}}{n}+\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{2}}{n}+\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{n}\right]

\displaystyle=w_{b,\infty,n}+T_{1}+\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}{n}\right)^{-1}\left(T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4}\right)

(D.8)

Rest of the proof is devoted to show
\left\|\hat{\omega}_{b,\infty,n}-\omega_{b,\infty,n}\right\|=o_{p}\left(K^{-3/2}\right)
via bounding
T_{1},T_{2},T_{3}
and
T_{4}
.

D.1
Bounding
T_{1}

To bound
T_{1}
, we first bound
\|\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0}\|
using the following Lemma:

Lemma D.1
.

Under assumptions
3.1
-
3.4
we have
\|\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0}\|=o_{p}\left(K^{-3/2}\right)\,.

The proof of Lemma
D.1
is similar to the proof of matrix convergence portion of the proof of Theorem
3.6
and is deferred to Section
E
. We now show that the operator norm of
(\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{k}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{k}/n)^{-1}
is bounded above. Using a conditional version of Theorem
F.4
of Section
F
we have:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{k}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{k}}{n}-\mathbb{E}\left(\tilde{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})\tilde{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}|\leq\tau}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)\right\|_{op}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right]\leq C\left(\frac{K\log{K}}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{K\log{K}}{n}}\right)

As the bound on the right side does not depend on
\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})
, we conclude, taking expectation on the both side:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{k}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{k}}{n}-\mathbb{E}\left(\tilde{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})\tilde{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}|\leq\tau}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)\right\|_{op}\right]\leq C\left(\frac{K\log{K}}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{K\log{K}}{n}}\right)

(D.9)

Note that we can write with
a_{n}=\log{n}/\sqrt{n}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\tilde{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})\tilde{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}|\leq\tau}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\tilde{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})\tilde{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}|\leq\tau}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\mathbb{E}\left(\tilde{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})\tilde{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}|\leq\tau}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|>a_{n}}\,.

As both of the matrices on the right side are p.s.d., we conclude:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\tilde{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})\tilde{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}|\leq\tau}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\geq\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\tilde{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})\tilde{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}|\leq\tau}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}\right)\,.

Choose
\delta>0
such that
\mathbb{P}(\|z\|\leq\delta)>0
. Now, from Theorem
F.2
:

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\tilde{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})\tilde{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}|\leq\tau}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq 1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\kappa_{4}\min_{|x|\leq\tau}f_{\eta+(\gamma_{0}-\hat{\gamma}_{n})^{\top}Z}(x)\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle\geq\kappa_{4}\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}|x|\leq\tau\\
\|a\|\leq a_{n}\end{subarray}}f_{\eta+a^{\top}Z}(x)\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{4}\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}|x|\leq\tau\\
\|a\|\leq a_{n}\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}f_{\eta}(x-a^{\top}z)f_{Z}(z)\ dz\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle\geq\kappa_{4}\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}|x|\leq\tau\\
\|a\|\leq a_{n}\end{subarray}}\int_{\|z\|\leq\delta}f_{\eta}(x-a^{\top}z)f_{Z}(z)\ dz\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}

\displaystyle\geq\kappa_{4}\min_{|x|\leq\tau+a_{n}\delta}f_{\eta}(x)P(\|Z\|\leq\delta)\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq a_{n}}\,.

Now for large
n
,
a_{n}\delta\leq\xi
(where
\xi
is same as defined in (iv) of Assumption
3.2
) and hence for all large
n
:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\tilde{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})\tilde{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}|\leq\tau}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq 1}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\geq\kappa_{4}\min_{|x|\leq\tau+{\epsilon}}f_{\eta}(x)P(\|Z\|\leq\delta)\mathds{1}_{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq 1}\,.

(D.10)

From equation (
D.9
) and (
D.1
) we conclude:

\left\|\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{k}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{k}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{op}=\left(\lambda_{\min}\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{k}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{k}}{n}\right)\right)^{-1}=O_{p}(1)\,.

(D.11)

We next also provide a bound Now going back to
T_{1}
in equation (
D.8
) we have:

\displaystyle\|T_{1}\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}X\left(\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0}\right)}{n}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}{n}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|_{op}\left\|\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}{n}\right)^{-1/2}\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}X\left(\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0}\right)}{n}\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{P}\left\|\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}{n}\right)^{-1/2}\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}X\left(\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0}\right)}{n}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{X(\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0})}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0}\|\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}(X^{\top}X/n)}\lesssim_{P}\|\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0}\|=o_{p}\left(K^{-3/2}\right)\hskip 14.45377pt[\text{By Lemma }\ref{lem:beta_bound}]\,.

D.2
Bounding
T_{2}

The term
T_{2}
can be bounded as:

\displaystyle\|T_{2}\|=\left\|\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{1}}{n}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{\mathbf{R}_{1}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\lambda_{\max}\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}{n}\right)

It was already proved in equation (
D.11
) that
\lambda_{\max}(\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}/n)=O_{p}(1)
. To control
\left\|\mathbf{R}_{1}\right\|/\sqrt{n}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\|\mathbf{R}_{1}\|^{2}}{n}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left((b(\eta)-b(\hat{\eta}))^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{D}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\left(b^{\prime}(\eta)(\hat{\eta}-\eta)+(1/2)b^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\eta})(\hat{\eta}-\eta)^{2}\right)^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|^{2}\mathbb{E}\left(\left(b^{\prime}(\eta)\|Z\|\right)^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\|b^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|^{4}\mathbb{E}((\|Z\|^{4}))

\displaystyle=O_{p}(n^{-1})\,.

Hence
\|\mathbf{R}_{1}/\sqrt{n}\|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})=o_{p}(K^{-3/2})
, where the last equality follows from Remark
2.2
.

D.3
Bounding
T_{3}

\displaystyle\|T_{3}\|=\left\|\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{2}}{n}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{\mathbf{R}_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\lambda_{\max}\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}{n}\right)

With similar logic used in bounding
T_{2}
, all we need to bound
\|\mathbf{R}_{2}\|/\sqrt{n}
. It is immediate that:

\displaystyle\frac{\|\mathbf{R}_{2}\|}{\sqrt{n}}=\sqrt{\frac{\|\mathbf{R}_{2}\|^{2}}{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{|x|\leq\tau}\left|b(t)-\tilde{N}_{K}(t)^{\top}\omega_{b,2,\infty}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{2\tau}{K}\right)^{3}\|b^{\prime\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}=o_{p}\left(K^{-3/2}\right)\,.

D.4
Bounding
T_{4}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left\|\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{n}\right\|^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}{\sf tr}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\epsilon}}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}{\sf tr}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left({\bm{\epsilon}}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathbf{Z},\bm{\eta},\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K+3}{n}\sup_{\eta}{\sf var}\left({\epsilon}\,\middle|\,\eta\right)\lambda_{\max}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{N}_{K}(\hat{\eta})\tilde{N}_{K}(\hat{\eta})^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}|\leq\tau}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\right]\right)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\frac{K}{n}\right)=o_{p}\left(K^{-3/2}\right)\hskip 14.45377pt[\text{Remark }\ref{rem:K}]\,.

where
\bm{\eta}
is
\{\eta_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n/3}
in
\mathcal{D}_{2}
and the penultimate inequality follows from Lemma
E.1
. These bounds established
\|\hat{\omega}_{b,n,\infty}-\omega_{b,\infty}\|=o_{p}\left(K^{-3/2}\right)
which completes the proof.

Appendix E
Proof of supplementary lemmas

E.1
Proof of Lemma
B.1

The definition of function
g
is as follows:

g(a,t)=\mathbb{E}\left[\begin{pmatrix}S&X&b^{\prime}(\eta)Z\end{pmatrix}\,\middle|\,\eta+a^{\top}Z=t\right]

Note that
g(a,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{1+p_{1}+p_{2}}
. Divide the components of
g
in three parts as follows:

1.

g_{1}(a,t)=\mathbb{E}\left[S\,\middle|\,\eta+a^{\top}Z=t\right]
.

2.

g_{2}(a,t)=\mathbb{E}\left[X\,\middle|\,\eta+a^{\top}Z=t\right]
.

3.

g_{3}(a,t)=\mathbb{E}\left[b^{\prime}(\eta)Z\,\middle|\,\eta+a^{\top}Z=t\right]
.

If we prove the continuity of partial derivates of
g_{1},g_{2},g_{3}
separately then we are done. We start with
g_{1}
. For fixed
a
(i.e. we consider the partial derivative with respect to
t
):

\displaystyle g_{1}(a,t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[S\,\middle|\,\eta+a^{\top}Z=t\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{z:t>(a+\gamma_{0})^{\top}z}f_{Z}(z)f_{\eta}(t-a^{\top}z)\ dz}{f_{\eta+Z^{\top}a}(t)}

\displaystyle\triangleq\frac{\int_{\Omega(t)}f_{Z}(z)f_{\eta}(t-a^{\top}z)\ dz}{f_{\eta+Z^{\top}a}(t)}

By using Leibnitz rule for differentiating integral with varying domain, we can immediately conclude
\partial_{t}g(a,t)
is continuously differentiable. The calculation for
\partial_{a}g(a,t)
is similar and hence skipped for brevity.

For
g_{2}
define
h(Z)=E\left(X\,\middle|\,Z\right)
. Then we have:

\displaystyle g_{2}(a,t)=\mathbb{E}\left[h(Z)\,\middle|\,\eta+a^{\top}Z=t\right]=\frac{\int h(z)f_{Z}(z)f_{\eta}(t-a^{\top}z)\ dz}{\int f_{Z}(z)f_{\eta}(t-a^{\top}z)\ dz}

That
g_{2}
is continuous both with respect to
a
and
t
follows from DCT and the fact that
E(\|h(Z)\|)<\infty
(Assumption
3.4
) and
\|f_{\eta}\|_{\infty}
is finite (Assumption
3.2
). The differentiability and continuity of the derivative also follows from the fact that
f_{\eta}
is differentiable and
\mathbb{E}(\|Z\|\|h(Z)\|)<\infty
as well as
\mathbb{E}(\|Z\|)<\infty
. Also note that differentiation under integral sign is allowed as
\mathbb{E}(\|h(Z)\|)<\infty
.

Finally for
g_{3}
define
h_{1}(Z)=b^{\prime}(t-a^{\top}Z)Z
. Then we have:

\displaystyle g_{3}(a,t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[h_{1}(Z)\,\middle|\,\eta+a^{\top}Z=t\right]

By the same logic as for
g_{2}
(i.e. using
E(\|Z\||b^{\prime}(t-a^{\top}Z)|)<\infty,E(\|Z\|^{2}|b^{\prime}(t-a^{\top}Z)|)<\infty
and
\|f_{\eta}\|_{\infty}<\infty
) our conclusion follows. Finally the continuity of
V(a,t)
follows directly from the continuity of density of
\eta
(Assumption
3.2
) and
(X,Z)
(Assumption
3.4
).

E.2
Proof of Lemma
D.1

Recall that
\beta_{0}
in this proof is
(\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}^{\top})^{\top}
and
X=(S,X)\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{2}\times(1+p_{1})}
as mentioned in the beginning of the proof of Proposition
2.1
. From the definition of
\hat{\beta}
(equation (
D.5
)):

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}

\displaystyle=\left(X^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}X\right)^{-1}X^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}Y

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{X^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}X}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{X^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}Y}{n}

\displaystyle=\beta_{0}+\left(\frac{X^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}X}{n}\right)^{-1}\left[\frac{X^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{R}_{1}}{n}+\frac{X^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{R}_{2}}{n}+\frac{X^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{n}\right]

We divide the entire proof into few steps which we articulate below first:

1.

First we show the matrix
X^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}X/n=O_{p}(1)
. More specifically we show that:

\frac{X^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}X}{n}\overset{P}{\longrightarrow}\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{E}\left[(X-\mathbb{E}(X\,\middle|\,\eta)(X-\mathbb{E}(X\,\middle|\,\eta))^{\top})\mathds{1}_{|\eta|\leq\tau}\right]\,.

This along with Assumption
3.3
. implies that
(X^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}X/n)^{-1}=O_{p}(1)
.

2.

Next we show that the residual terms are negligible:

\left(\frac{X^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}X}{n}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{X^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}(\mathbf{R}_{1}+\mathbf{R}_{2})}{n}\right)=o_{p}(K^{-3/2})\,.

3.

Finally we show that
\mathbf{X}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}{\bm{\epsilon}}/n=o_{p}(K^{-3/2})
. This will complete the proof.

Proof of Step 1:
Recall the definition of
\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}
from Subsection
B.1
. It then follows immediately that:

X=\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}\begin{bmatrix}e_{1}&e_{2}&\dots&e_{1+p_{1}}\end{bmatrix}:=\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1}A

where
e_{i}
is the
i^{th}
canonical basis of
\mathbb{R}^{(1+p_{1}+p_{2})}
. Hence we have:

\frac{X^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}X}{n}=A^{\top}\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*^{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*}}{n}A

As established in Subsection
B.1
(see equation (
B.5
)):

\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*^{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*}}{n}\overset{P}{\to}\frac{1}{3}\Omega_{\tau}\,,

we conclude from that:

\displaystyle\frac{X^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}X}{n}

\displaystyle\overset{P}{\longrightarrow}\frac{1}{3}A^{\top}\Omega_{\tau}A

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(X-\mathbb{E}(X\,\middle|\,\eta)\right)\left(X-\mathbb{E}(X\,\middle|\,\eta)\right)^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\eta|\leq\tau}\right]\,.

(E.1)

Proof of Step 2:

For the first residual term observe that:

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{X}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{R}_{1}}{n}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{X}}{n}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|_{op}\left\|\left(\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{X}}{n}\right)^{-1/2}\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{R}_{1}}{n}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{X}}{n}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|_{op}\left\|\frac{\mathbf{R}_{1}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|=o_{p}(K^{-3/2})\,.

(E.2)

The last equality follows from the fact that the first term of the above product is
O_{p}(1)
and the second term
\|\mathbf{R}_{1}\|/\sqrt{n}
is
o_{p}(K^{-3/2})
because:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{1}}{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\left(b(\eta_{i})-b(\hat{\eta}_{i})\right)^{2}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\left(\eta_{i}-\hat{\eta}_{i}\right)^{2}(b^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}_{i}))^{2}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau}+\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\left(\eta_{i}-\hat{\eta}_{i}\right)^{4}(b^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\eta}_{i}))^{2}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau}

\displaystyle\leq\|b^{\prime}\|_{\infty,[-\tau,\tau]}\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|^{2}\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\|Z_{i}\|^{2}+\|b^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|^{4}\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/3}\|Z_{i}\|^{4}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(n^{-1})+O_{p}(n^{-2})=o_{p}(K^{-3})\hskip 14.45377pt[\text{Remark }\ref{rem:K}]\,.

For the other residual using the same calculation we first conclude:

\left\|\left(\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{X}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{R}_{2}}{n}\right\|\leq\left\|\left(\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}\mathbf{X}}{n}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|_{op}\left\|\frac{\mathbf{R}_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|

(E.3)

and
\|\mathbf{R}_{2}\|/\sqrt{n}=o_{p}(K^{-3/2})
follows directly from equation (
D.2
).

Proof of Step 3:

Finally we show that
\mathbf{X}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}{\bm{\epsilon}}/n=o_{p}(1)
which completes the proof. Recall that, in Subsection
B.1
, we show that the term
T_{1}=
. This immediately implies:

\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*^{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{n}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})\,.

which, in turn implies:

\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{n}=A^{\top}\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}^{*^{\top}}{\sf proj}^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{K}}{\bm{\epsilon}}}{n}A=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})=o_{p}(K^{-3/2})\hskip 7.22743pt[\text{Remark }\ref{rem:K}]\,.

(E.4)

Combining equation (
E.1
), (
E.2
), (
E.3
) and (
E.4
) we conclude
\|\hat{\beta}-\beta_{0}\|=o_{p}(K^{-3/2})
.

E.3
Some auxiliary lemmas

In this section we present some auxiliary lemmas that are necessary to establish our main results.

Lemma E.1
.

Suppose
A
is a p.s.d. matrix and
B
is a symmetric matrix, then
{\sf tr}(AB)\leq\lambda_{\max}(B){\sf tr}(A)
.

Proof.

Note that
B-\lambda_{\max}(B)I\preceq 0
. Hence:

{\sf tr}\left(A(\lambda_{\max}(B)I-B)\right)=\lambda_{\max}(B){\sf tr}(A)-{\sf tr}(AB)\geq 0\,.

as
A(\lambda_{\max}(B)I-B)
is a p.s.d. matrix.
∎

Lemma E.2
.

Suppose
\{X_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a sequence of non-negative random variables and
\{\mathcal{F}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a sequence of sigma fields. If
\mathbb{E}\left(X_{n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}\right)=o_{p}(1)
, then
X_{n}=o_{p}(1)
.

Proof.

Fix
{\epsilon}>0
. From a conditional version of Markov inequality, we have:

Y_{n}:=\mathbb{P}\left(X_{n}>{\epsilon}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}\right)\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left(X_{n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}\right)}{{\epsilon}}=o_{p}(1)\,.

Now as
\{Y_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded sequence of random variables which converge to
0
in probability, applying DCT we conclude:

\mathbb{P}\left(X_{n}>{\epsilon}\right)=\mathbb{E}(Y_{n})=o(1)\,.

This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix F
Some preliminary discussion on B-spline basis

Recall that, we have mentioned in Section
2
of the main document that we use truncated B-spline basis to approximate both the unknown mean function
b(\eta)=\mathbb{E}(\nu\mid\eta)
and its derivative. More specifically, we fit spline basis on
[-\tau_{n},\tau_{n}]
and then and define our estimator to be
0
outside of it. Recall that the B-spline basis starts with
0^{th}
order polynomial (i.e. a constant functions) and then is recursively defined for higher order polynomial. Let the knots inside
[-\tau_{n},\tau_{n}]
are:

-\tau_{n}=\xi_{0}<\xi_{1}<\xi_{2}<\dots<\xi_{K}=\tau_{n}

As we know from spline theory, the dimension of the space generated by spline basis of degree
s
is
K+s
. When
s=0
, (i.e. constant functions) we need
K
basis functions which are defined as:

N_{i,0}(t)=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }\xi_{i}\leq t\leq\xi_{i+1}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\,.

for
0\leq i\leq K-1
. Now we define the recursion, i.e. how we go to a collection of B-spline basis functions of degree
p
degree from a collection of B-spline basis functions of degree
(p-1)
. Note that we need
K+p
many basis functions of degree
p
and each of the basis functions will be local in a sense that they only have support over
p+1
many intervals (observe that the constant functions are only supported over one interval). For that we first append some knots at the both ends. For example, to go to
1
degree polynomial basis from
0
-degree, we append two knots, one at the beginning and one at the very end.

-\tau_{n}=\xi_{-1}=\xi_{0}<\xi_{1}<\dots<\xi_{K}=\xi_{K+1}=\tau_{n}\,.

Our
k+1
basis functions are defined as:

N_{i,1}(t)=\begin{cases}\frac{t-\xi_{i}}{\xi_{i+1}-\xi_{i}}N_{i,0}(t)+\frac{\xi_{i+2}-t}{\xi_{i+2}-\xi_{i+1}}N_{(i+1),0}(t)&\text{if }\xi_{i}\leq t\leq\xi_{i+2}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\,.

for
-1\leq i\leq K-1
. Note that when
i=-1
,
N_{-1,0}
does not exist. Hence for that case we forget this part and define:

N_{-1,1}(t)=\begin{cases}\frac{\xi_{1}-t}{\xi_{1}-\xi_{0}}N_{0,0}(t)&\text{if }\xi_{-1}\leq t\leq\xi_{1}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\,.

and the for the last basis, i.e.
i=K-1
,
B_{K,0}
is not defined. Hence analogously we define:

N_{K-1,1}(t)=\begin{cases}\frac{t-\xi_{K-1}}{\xi_{K}-\xi_{K-1}}N_{K-1,0}(t)&\text{if }\xi_{K-1}\leq t\leq\xi_{K}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\,.

Now we will extend this pattern for any general degree
p
. For that we need to append
p
knots at the both ends:

-\tau_{n}=\xi_{-p}=\cdots=\xi_{-1}=\xi_{0}<\xi_{1}<\dots<\xi_{K}=\xi_{K+1}=\dots\xi_{k+p}=\tau_{n}\,.

and the recursion is defined as:

N_{i,p}(t)=\begin{cases}\frac{t-\xi_{i}}{\xi_{i+p}-\xi_{i}}N_{i,(p-1)}(t)+\frac{\xi_{i+p+1}-t}{\xi_{i+p+1}-\xi_{i}}N_{(i+1),(p-1)}(t)&\text{if }\xi_{i}\leq t\leq\xi_{i+p+1}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\,.

for
-p\leq i\leq k-1
. Define a class of functions
\mathbb{S}_{p,k}
is the linear combinations of all the functions of
p^{th}
order B-spline basis
\{N_{i,p}\}_{i=-p}^{k-1}
, i.e.

\mathbb{S}_{p,k}=\left\{f:f(x)=\sum_{i=-p}^{k-1}c_{i}N_{i,p}(x)\text{ with }c_{-p},\dots,c_{k-1}\in\mathbb{R}\right\}\,.

The following theorem is Theorem 17 of Chapter 1 of
[
23
]
which provides an approximation error of a function (and its derivatives) with respect to B-spline basis:

Theorem F.1

(Functional approximation using B-spline basis)
.

For any
0\leq r\leq l\leq p
, if
\sup_{|x|\leq\tau}|f(x)|<\infty
and
f
is
(l+1)
times differentiable with
(l+1)^{th}
derivative also bounded on
[-\tau,\tau]
then we have:

\inf_{s\in\mathbb{S}_{p,k}}\left\|\partial_{r}f-\partial_{r}s\right\|_{\infty}\leq C\left(\frac{2\tau}{k}\right)^{(l+1-r)}\|f^{(l+1)}\|_{\infty}

where the constant
C
only depends on
p
, the order of the spline approximation.

In our paper, we also need to control the behavior of the lower eigenvalue of the population matrix
\mathbb{E}\left(N_{k}(\hat{\eta})N_{K}(\hat{\eta})^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}|\leq\tau}\mid\mathcal{D}_{1}\right)
. For that reason, we use a scaled version of B-spline basis instead:

\tilde{N}_{i,p}(x)=\sqrt{\frac{K}{2\tau}}N_{i,p}(x)\,.

and use the following theorem (see the theorem of section 3 of
[
14
]
or Theorem 11 of
[
23
]
):

Theorem F.2

(Eigenvalue of spline matrix)
.

Define a matrix
G\in\mathbb{R}^{(k+p)\times(k+p)}
such that:

G_{ij}=\int_{-\tau}^{\tau}\tilde{N}_{i,p}(x)\tilde{N}_{j,p}(x)\ dx

Then there exists a constant
\kappa_{p}>0
only depending on
p
such that:

\kappa_{p}\leq\frac{x^{\top}Gx}{x^{\top}x}\leq 1

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{(k+p)}
. In particular if
X\sim F
with density
f
on
[-\tau,\tau]
and if
0<f_{-}\leq f(x)\leq f_{+}<\infty
then we have:

\kappa_{p}f_{-}\leq\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\tilde{N}_{k}(X)\tilde{N}_{K}(X)^{\top}\right)\right)\leq\lambda_{\max}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\tilde{N}_{k}(X)\tilde{N}_{K}(X)^{\top}\right)\right)\leq f_{+}\,.

Remark F.3
.

Note that the linear span of
\{N_{i,p}\}_{i=1}^{(k+p)}
and
\{\tilde{N}_{i,p}\}_{i=1}^{(k+p)}
is same as
\mathbb{S}_{p,k}
because
N_{i,p}
and
\tilde{N}_{i,p}
only differs by a scaling constant. Hence Theorem
F.1
remains unaltered even if we use the scaled B-spline basis
\{\tilde{N}_{i,p}\}_{i=1}^{(k+p)}
.

Another result which is due of
[
36
]
, is used in this paper to bound the minimum eigenvalue (e.g. the operator norm of the inverse) of the sample covariance matrix formed by
\{\tilde{N}_{K}(\hat{\eta}_{i})\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}_{i}|\leq\tau}\}_{i=1}^{n}
in terms of the population covariance matrix
\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})\tilde{N}_{k}(\hat{\eta})^{\top}\mathds{1}_{|\hat{\eta}|\leq\tau}\right]
is the following (see Lemma 6.2 of
[
4
]
):

Theorem F.4
.

Let
Q_{1},\dots,Q_{n}
be independent symmetric non-negative
k\times k
matrix-valued random variable. Let
\bar{Q}=(1/n)\sum_{i}Q_{i}
and
Q=E(\bar{Q})
. If
\|Q_{i}\|_{op}\leq M
a.s. then we have:

\mathbb{E}\left\|\bar{Q}-Q\right\|_{op}\leq C\left(\frac{M\log{k}}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{M\|Q\|_{op}\log{k}}{n}}\right)\,.

for some absolute constant
C
. In particular if
Q_{i}=p_{i}p_{i}^{\top}
for some random vector
p_{i}
with
\|p_{i}\|\leq\xi_{k}
almost surely, then:

\mathbb{E}\left\|\bar{Q}-Q\right\|_{op}\leq C\left(\frac{\xi_{k}^{2}\log{k}}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{k}^{2}\|Q\|_{op}\log{k}}{n}}\right)\,.

Lastly, as we are working on the derivative estimation of the mean function
b
, we need to have a bound on
\|\nabla\tilde{N}_{p,K}(x)\|
for all
|x|\leq\tau
, where
\nabla\tilde{N}_{p,K}(x)
is the vector of derivatives of
\{\tilde{N}_{i,k}(x)\}_{i=1}^{k+p}
. Towards that end, we prove the following lemma;

Lemma F.5
.

For all
|x|\leq\tau
, we have:
\|\nabla\tilde{N}_{p,K}(x)\|\leq C_{p}K\sqrt{K}
for some constant
C_{p}
depending only on the order of the spline basis
p
and
\tau
.

Proof.

Note that, the unscaled B-spline
N_{K}(x)
forms a partition of unity, i.e for any
x\in[-\tau,\tau]
, we have
\sum_{j=1}^{K+p}N_{j,k}(x)=1
and also, by definition, each
x
only contributed to finitely many (at-most
p
many) basis. Hence it is immediate that, for any
x\in[-\tau,\tau]
,
\|N_{K}(x)\|\lesssim 1
and consequently
\|\tilde{N}_{K}(x)\|\lesssim\sqrt{K}
. Now, from Theorem 3 of
[
23
]
we have for any
1\leq j\leq k+p
:

\frac{d}{dx}N_{j,p,K}(x)=\frac{K}{2\tau}\left(N_{j,p-1,K}(x)-N_{j+1,p-1,K}(x)\right)

Hence:

\frac{d}{dx}\tilde{N}_{j,p,K}(x)=\frac{K\sqrt{K}}{\tau\sqrt{\tau}}\left(N_{j,p-1,K}(x)-N_{j+1,p-1,K}(x)\right)

which along with with the partition of unity property of
\{N_{i,p}\}_{i=1}^{k+p}
implies
\|\nabla\tilde{N}_{p,K}(x)\|\leq C_{p}K\sqrt{K}
, which completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Appendix G
Main algorithm

In this Subsection, we present our estimation method of the treatment effect
\alpha_{0}
detailed in Section
2
of the main document in an algorithmic format.

1.

Divide the whole data into three equal parts:
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{1}\cup\mathcal{D}_{2}\cup\mathcal{D}_{3}
.

2.

Estimate
\gamma_{0}
from
\mathcal{D}_{1}
by doing OLS regression of
Q
on
Z
, i.e. set:

\hat{\gamma}_{n}=(Z^{\top}Z)^{-1}Z^{\top}Q\,.

3.

Replace
\eta_{i}
in equation (
2.1
) by
\hat{\eta}_{i}
using
\hat{\gamma}_{n}
obtained in the previous step (i.e. set
\hat{\eta}_{i}=Q_{i}-Z_{i}^{\top}\hat{\gamma}_{n}
where
\{Q_{i},Z_{i}\}
are in
\mathcal{D}_{2}
and
\hat{\gamma}_{n}
obtained in Step (b). Then estimate
b^{\prime}
from
\mathcal{D}_{2}
using equation (
2.1
) via spline estimation method.

4.

Estimate
\alpha_{0}
from
\mathcal{D}_{3}
using
\hat{\gamma}_{n}
and
\hat{b}^{\prime}
estimated in the previous two steps.

5.

Finally, do the above steps by rotating the datasets and combine them to gain efficiency.

Appendix H
Proofs of auxiliary lemmas of Theorem
4.10

Lemma
A.3
involves the rate of estimation of the the deviation of
\check{\mathbf{W}}
from
\tilde{\mathbf{W}}
. Note that, to establish the rate of convergence of a sequence of random variable, it is enough to establish the bound on the set of probability going to 1. More precisely suppose
\{A_{n}\}
is a sequence of events such that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}(A_{n})=1
. Then, if we have a sequence to random variable
\{X_{n}\}
and we wish to show that
X_{n}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}t_{n}
, then it suffices to show that on
A_{n}
as:

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{|X_{n}|}{t_{n}}>t\right)=\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{|X_{n}|}{t_{n}}>t,A_{n}\right)\,.

We will use this fact in our proof. More specifically, define the event
\Omega_{n}
as:

\Omega_{n}=\left\{\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|\leq C\sqrt{\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}}\right\}\,.

for some fixed large constant
C
. Then from the properties of Lasso, we have
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{n})\to 1
as
n\to\infty
(as this event if true as long as RE condition is satisfied, which approaches to 1 as
n
goes to infinity.) Therefore, by our previous argument, whenever we want to establish rate of some terms, it is enough to establish the rate on the event
\Omega_{n}
.

H.1
Proof of Lemma
A.3

Proof.

In this proof, we denote by
a_{n}=(\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0})/\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\|
. We start with with bounding the distance from
\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}
to
\mathbf{W}^{\perp}
.
Recall that the only difference between
\breve{\mathbf{W}}^{\perp}
and
\mathbf{W}^{\perp}
is in the last
p_{2}
columns where we replace the coefficients of
Z
by
b^{\prime}(\eta)
and consequently the difference is
Z(\hat{b}^{\prime}(\hat{\eta})-b^{\prime}(\eta))
. Therefore we have for any
p_{1}+2\leq j\leq 1+p_{1}+p_{2}
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\|\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j}^{\perp}-\mathbf{W}_{*,j}^{\perp}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\left\|\breve{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j}^{\perp}-\mathbf{W}_{*,j}^{\perp}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{ij}^{2}(\hat{b}^{\prime}(\hat{\eta})-b^{\prime}(\eta))^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{ij}^{2}(\hat{b}^{\prime}(\hat{\eta})-b^{\prime}(\hat{\eta}))^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{ij}^{2}(b^{\prime}(\hat{\eta})-b^{\prime}(\eta))^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\dot{r}^{2}_{n}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i,j}^{2}+\|b^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i,j}^{2}(\hat{\eta}_{i}-\eta_{i})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\dot{r}^{2}_{n}+\left\|\hat{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{0}\right\|^{2}\ \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i,j}^{2}\left(Z_{i}^{\top}a_{n}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\dot{r}^{2}_{n}+\frac{s_{\gamma}\log{p}}{n}\,.

(H.1)

We next bound
\mathbf{W}^{\perp}_{*,j}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j}
. For notational simplicity, we define
p=1+p_{1}+p_{2}
. Now for any
0\leq j\leq p
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}^{\perp}_{*,j}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{*,j}

\displaystyle=m_{j}(\bm{\eta})-P_{\mathbf{N}_{k}}\mathbf{W}_{*,j}

\displaystyle=m_{j}(\bm{\eta})-\check{m}_{j}(\hat{\bm{\eta}})+\check{m}_{j}(\hat{\bm{\eta}})-P_{\mathbf{N}_{k}}\left(\check{m}_{j}\left(\hat{\bm{\eta}}\right)+\check{\nu}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\left[m_{j}(\bm{\eta})-\check{m}_{j}(\hat{\bm{\eta}})\right]+\mathbf{N}_{k}(\hat{\bm{\eta}})\ \check{\omega}_{j}+\check{\mathbf{R}}_{j}-P_{\mathbf{N}_{k}}\left(\mathbf{N}_{k}(\hat{\bm{\eta}})\ \check{\omega}_{j}+\check{\mathbf{R}}_{j}+\check{\nu}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\left[m_{j}(\bm{\eta})-\check{m}_{j}(\hat{\bm{\eta}})\right]+P^{\perp}_{\mathbf{N}_{k}}\check{\mathbf{R}}_{j}-P_{\mathbf{N}_{k}}\check{\nu}_{j}\,,

(H.2)

where
\check{\mathbf{R}}_{j}=\check{m}_{j}(\hat{\bm{\eta}})-\mathbf{N}_{K}(\hat{\bm{\eta}})\check{\omega}_{j}
is the B-spline approximation error on
[-\tau,\tau]
(recall that we are only using the observations in this interval) and both the notations
\widehat{\tilde{W}}_{*,j}
and
\check{\nu}_{j}
are used interchangeably to denote
